# Changelog

All notable changes to Token-Goat are documented in this file. Format follows Keep a Changelog. Token-Goat follows Semantic Versioning starting at 1.0.

## [Unreleased]

### Fixed

- **A suggested command is removed, and none of its path left behind, when the path holds a backtick or `$(`.** Single quotes keep both as written in bash and PowerShell, but token-goat sets each suggested command between backticks, and a backtick in the path closed that span early, so the model got a command cut off inside its quotes followed by the rest of the path as loose text. A `$(` runs a command if the suggestion is ever retyped without its quotes. Such a command is now removed and the sentence around it kept, as was already done for a double-quoted path. Removing a command that was not set between backticks also took only part of it, leaving the end of the path behind; it now takes the whole command. A path holding a plain `$`, such as `src/gen$api.ts`, keeps its commands.
- **The edit-anyway and truncated-read denies keep their commands when a file name holds `$`.** The deny for a large file ends with a `token-goat replace` command for editing it anyway, and the deny for a cut-off read suggests `skeleton` and `read`. Both put the path in double quotes, where `$` and a backtick are shell substitutions, so the safety check that removes unsafe commands left `src/gen$api.ts`'s deny saying "To edit it anyway, use `token-goat (command omitted: ...)`) to rewrite the whole file". A command with several arguments now quotes all of them with the same mark, single quotes when the path needs them, so the command survives and runs as written in bash and PowerShell. The same applies to the section, symbol and key commands the read hints and the grep and section hints suggest.
- **The first-read deny for large markdown files counts every heading and waits for the index.** With `hints.first_read_symbol_policy = "deny"`, a 401-heading guide was described as having 40 headings, because the count came from a list that stops at 40. The deny also fired on markdown files token-goat had never indexed or that had changed since, where the policy leaves code files alone because there is no index to read them from surgically. It now reports the index's own heading count and, like the code policy, stands aside for an unindexed or since-edited file, which still gets its heading list instead. A `hints.first_read_symbol_bytes` of `0` now turns it off for markdown as it already did for code.
- **Quiet hours no longer switch off `hints.first_read_symbol_policy = "deny"`.** Inside the `hints.quiet_hours` window the policy let every whole-file first read through, even set to `deny`, while every other deny token-goat makes, including the policy's own deny for large markdown files, stays in force at any hour. Quiet hours now only hold back the `warn` advice.
- **The first-read policy keeps its advice when a file name holds `$`.** Its deny and warning put the path in double quotes, where `$` and a backtick are shell substitutions, so the safety check that removes unsafe commands removed them together with the rest of the line: what was left of `src/gen$api.ts`'s deny was a broken sentence telling you to rewrite the whole file. Such a path is now single-quoted, which both bash and PowerShell keep as written, and the explanation sits on a line of its own, so the size, the policy name and the `outline` and `skeleton` alternatives all survive. The same applies to the deny for a large markdown file.
- **`hints.first_read_symbol_policy` now acts on indexed source files.** Setting it to `deny` or `warn` changed nothing for a large source file: the check that the file had not changed since it was indexed compared a time in seconds with one in milliseconds, so every indexed file looked out of date and every whole-file first read went through untouched. Both times are now in seconds, so `deny` blocks a whole-file first read of a large indexed file and `warn` suggests a surgical read. A file whose timestamp moved but whose contents did not, as after switching branches and back, still counts as indexed.
- **Pulling or rebasing in a linked worktree no longer moves your installed token-goat onto that worktree's build.** The git hook that runs after a pull rebuilds the bundle and then reinstalls the hooks and restarts the worker. Run in a second worktree, it pointed every installed hook at that worktree, so deleting the worktree later left the hooks pointing at a folder that was gone. In a linked worktree it now only builds, and says to run `npm run sync` from the main checkout to install a new build.
- **A hook on a busy machine no longer drops the payload it was sent.** A hook waits up to five seconds for its input. If the machine was too busy to run the hook during that time, it woke up, saw its own timer had expired, and gave up, even though the input was already there to read. It then passed silently: the read went unrecorded, and no hint or repeat-read guard fired for that call. The hook now reads whatever input has arrived before it gives up.
- **A default-exported options object or factory call is indexed as `default`.** A Vite config written as `export default defineConfig({ ... })`, a Vue component written as `export default { ... }` or `export default defineComponent({ ... })`, and a TypeScript `export default { ... } satisfies Config` all left `symbol default` saying "No matches", so the file's main export could only be read by line number. Each is now indexed as `default`: a `variable` in a `.ts` or `.js` file, a `sfc_script_const` in a `.vue` file. `export default foo` still adds nothing, since `foo` is already indexed under its own name. The change is in the shared parser, so upgrading reparses already-indexed files once.
- **`pdf-locate` names only the pages it searched when `--max-matches` stops it early.** `pdf-locate three.pdf alpha --pages 1-5 --max-matches 1` on a three-page PDF stopped after a match on page 1, yet its note said `showing pages 1-3`, as if pages 2 and 3 had been searched too. The note now reads `searched page 1 before --max-matches stopped the scan`. A scan that reaches the last page keeps the old note, and `pdf-extract` is unchanged. The note lives in the PDF reader, so upgrading re-embeds already-indexed PDFs once; no other file type is affected.
- **`read` and `symbol` keep a doc comment's bare first line.** A comment run that opens with a line holding only `//` or `#`, such as `//` then `// Deletes the account.`, was printed starting from its second line, so the doc comment shown above a function was not the one in the file. The whole run is printed now. A stray `//` above a `/** ... */` comment is still left out, since it is not part of that comment.
- **`find` and `locate` escape the pattern they repeat back.** When nothing matched exactly, `find` and `locate` named your pattern and the nearest names they fell back to exactly as typed, so a pattern holding a line break started a new line that could pass for one of token-goat's own. `find` also printed a file named `[tg] note.ts` unchanged, where `locate` already escaped it. Line breaks and other control characters in these now show as escapes such as `\n`, the same way file names already do.
- **`symbol` shows the doc comment above each match, as `read` does.** `read "file::name"` prints the doc comment directly above a function, but `symbol name` printed only the first five lines of the body, so a warning in the doc never appeared there. Each match now prints the doc comment's own lines before the body preview, and the header counts them (`+ 4-line doc comment`). The doc lines are extra, so you still see five lines of the body.
- **`config validate` reports a `config.toml` value that does not take effect.** `overflow_guard.max_tokens` has a floor of 1000, so `max_tokens = 500` ran as 1000, while `config validate` said "no issues found". The same thing happened to any setting outside its allowed range or of the wrong type. `validate` now lists each one as `value_ignored`, with the reason and the value actually in use, such as `500 is outside the allowed range 1000-1000000; in effect: 1000`. It exits 1, like it does for a bad value in a project file or an environment variable.
- **Your own query and `--grep` pattern are escaped when a message repeats them.** `semantic` quotes your query when it finds nothing, when `--exclude-tests` hides every match, and above each block of a multi-query run. Many listing commands quote your `--grep` pattern when it matched none of the results. Those messages printed the text as you typed it, so a query holding a line break started a new line that could pass for one of token-goat's own. Line breaks and other control characters now show as escapes such as `\n`, the same way file names already do.
- **`search` now counts what it shows, and marks results from deleted files.** The header lists how many results each channel found, such as `[symbol:1, heading:1, ...]`. Those counts included hits that `--limit` had already cut, so with `--limit 1` the header could name two channels above a single result. The counts now cover the results shown. A result from a file that is no longer on disk, such as a deleted worktree the index still remembers, could also rank first with nothing to say the file was gone. It now sorts after every live result and carries the same `⚠ DELETED` tag `symbol` and `refs` use, with `deleted: true` in `--json`.
- **`pr-slice --json` comments now check the quoted diff, not only the comment.** Each review comment carries the lines of the diff it was left on. That diff was written by the pull request's author, so it can carry the same planted instructions as a comment. The JSON output checked only the comment text and passed the diff through as plain text. Now a diff that looks like an instruction gets the same warning fence as a comment would.
- **Fenced output now stays inside the output cap.** `html-query`, `html-outline`, `pr-slice`, `dep-docs`, `zip-list`, and `zip-read` wrap what they print in a fence that marks it as outside content. The fence's tags and notice were added after the cap had already given the whole `overflow_guard.max_tokens` budget to the text, so the output could run past the cap. A long HTML page came out at about 1,029 tokens under a 1,000-token cap, and a text just under the cap went out uncut and over it once fenced. The fence is now counted inside the cap.
- **A `--limit` of 0 and a negative one get the same message.** `token-goat symbol x --limit -1` said the value "must be a non-negative number", which suggested 0 would work, but `--limit 0` then said it "must be a positive number, got: 0". The same split hit `semantic`, `brief`, `refs`, `find`, `locate`, `callers`, `types`, `hot`, `history`, `recent`, and `--depth` on `call-chain` and `affected`. Both values now get one message, "must be a positive number", with the value in quotes. `docx-tables --table` also no longer accepts a value with trailing letters such as `2x`.
- **Reads from before a compaction no longer count against a Read after it.** Claude Code can run hooks in more than one process, and a process that had not yet seen a compaction recorded its reads without noting how many came before. A source file read once since the compaction could then be refused as if all its earlier reads still counted. A file read four times before a compaction had also lost its recent-read protection for good, so it was refused again after only two reads since the compaction. Only reads since the last compaction count now.
- **A Read refused because nothing changed no longer counts as another read.** Each time a Read of an unchanged file was refused, the file's read count still went up, though the model got nothing new. A refusal after two reads said "Tried to read this file 3 times already", and the next one said 4. The session manifest kept the same inflated re-read count. Only a read that hands over the file, or a diff of what changed in it, counts now.
- **A suggested command runs as printed.** After `rg "DeveloperStatus|ContractStatus" f.ts`, the hint suggested `token-goat symbol DeveloperStatus|ContractStatus`, which a shell runs as a pipe into a command named `ContractStatus`. It now suggests `token-goat symbol "DeveloperStatus"` or `token-goat symbol "ContractStatus"`. Skill names in the `skill-body`, `skill-section` and `skill-compact` suggestions are quoted the same way. A whole command named inside a sentence, such as "Run 'token-goat doctor --fix'", was wrapped in quotes that read as part of the command when copied; it is now set in backticks.
- **`doctor --fix` no longer lists a settings change it could not save.** When the config file could not be written, for example because it was read-only, `token-goat doctor --fix` still listed the setting it meant to change under "Repairs applied", and with `network.offline` among them it went on to try the model download anyway. The error now names each setting that was not applied, and nothing that depends on those settings is attempted.
- **`upgrade --check` and `doctor --fix` fail when they could not do their job.** With `network.offline` set, or a registry that gave no usable version, `token-goat upgrade --check` printed a `[!]` line and succeeded, so a script could not tell a failed check from an answer. It now exits 1 with the reason on stderr, and `--json` still prints the status, `error` field included. `token-goat doctor --fix` listed the repairs it could not make in its report and still succeeded; they now go to stderr after the report, and the run exits 1.
- **`refs` with `--grep` gives the same answer for one name or several.** `token-goat refs used --grep nomatch` said the only reference was filtered out and succeeded, but `token-goat refs used,unusedB --grep nomatch` printed the same note as an error and failed. Several names now succeed too when each was either found or filtered out, and `refs "a.ts::x,b.ts::y" --json --grep` now says how many references the filter hid for each name, as the one-file form already did. A note about a single filtered item now reads "the only reference was filtered out ... to see it" instead of "all 1 reference was filtered out", and the same goes for `outline`, `csv-query`, `trace`, `context-for --budget` (which also said "1 tokens") and `failures --delta`.
- **A suggested `config-get` quotes its key, and a key holding a double quote is no longer suggested.** The hints for an `.env`, `.properties` or `tsconfig.json` file printed `token-goat config-get ".env" PORT`, with the key left bare after the quoted path, unlike every other suggested argument. The key is now quoted too. A YAML key such as `'say "hi"'`, or a TOML key written `"my key"`, was offered as `yaml-query "keys.yaml" "say "hi""`, which a shell splits and which fails with "key 'say hi' does not exist"; the hint now names the next key in the file instead.
- **A path with spaces that the shell split apart gets the right suggestion.** `token-goat skeleton my proj/src dir/big file.ts`, typed without quotes, reaches token-goat as four separate arguments, and the note about the extra arguments suggested joining them with commas: `token-goat skeleton "my,proj/src,dir/big,file.ts"`, a path that does not exist. When the words joined back with spaces name a real file, the note now suggests that path in quotes. The same goes for `read`, `section` and the other commands that read one file or spec, including a section heading with a space in it.
- **A suggested command keeps a `$` or a quote in its argument.** After `token-goat symbol '$ref'` missed, it suggested `token-goat semantic "$ref"`, and bash and PowerShell both expand `$ref` inside double quotes, so the suggestion searched for nothing. An `answer` refusal over a question holding a quoted path nested one pair of double quotes inside another. A suggested argument holding `$`, a backtick or a double quote is now wrapped in single quotes, which both shells pass through unchanged, and every other argument keeps its double quotes. The MCP server still turns such a retry into "call the tool again with this parameter".
- **`answer` finds a file whose path holds a space.** `token-goat answer 'what does "src dir/big file.ts" export'` refused with "not an indexed symbol or file" for a file the index held, because any question naming something with a space in it was turned away before the lookup. It now answers with that file's exports, and its `via:` line quotes the path.
- **In bypassPermissions mode, a rewrite is no longer approved past a rule source the check could not see.** A skill whose `disallowed-tools` removes a command by pattern still let token-goat approve its rewritten command when the skill sat inside `node_modules` outside a git checkout, behind a symlink or a Windows junction, under a folder git ignores while the folder holding its `.claude` is not (Claude Code still loads it there), or when it was written to `~/.claude` or the project's `.claude` less than a minute earlier. A call made inside a custom subagent was approved too, though the subagent's own definition can remove tools by pattern. Each of these now leaves the call alone. The resident hook server now reads the claude process's command line without blocking (the read takes about a second on Windows), so a hook call that arrives meanwhile is handed to another server straight away. [docs/security.md](docs/security.md) names the two unseen sources that can still let a call be approved: a skill nested deeper in the project written within the last minute, and remote control turned on after the session started.
- **"Did you mean" no longer suggests a symbol that is gone.** After a function was renamed in a file edited outside the index, or its file was deleted, `symbol` still offered the old name, and `read`, `brief` and `refs` still pointed at the old file, so following the suggestion ended in another "No matches". Each suggestion is now checked first: a changed file is reindexed before the suggestions are worked out, and a symbol whose file was deleted is no longer offered.
- **Swift members written on one line with their type are all indexed.** In `struct STwo { var a = 0; var b = 1; func f() -> Int { return 1 } }` only the type and `a` were indexed, so `symbol f` said "No matches". The same went for one-line classes, protocols, enums, extensions and actors, for a second method on the `class SThree {` line, for a nested type closed on that line, and for a second member sharing a body line with the first (`func c() {}; func d() {}`). Each member is now indexed under its type with its own lines, and `read "file::f"` prints just that member.
- **C# members written on one line with their type are indexed.** In `class COne { public int Ca() { return 1; } public int P { get; set; } }` only the class was indexed, so `symbol Ca` said "No matches". The same went for one-line structs, interfaces and records, for a constructor or method on the `class CTwo {` line, for a nested class closed on that line, and for a second member sharing a body line with the first (`public int Cb() => 2; public int Cc() => 3;`), including events, finalizers, operators and indexers. Each member is now indexed under its type with its own lines, and `read "file::Ca"` prints just that member.
- **Scala members written on one line with their type are indexed.** In `object STwo { def sa(): Int = 1; val sv = 2 }` only the object was indexed, so `symbol sa` said "No matches". The same went for one-line classes, traits, enums and `given ... with { ... }` instances, for a method on the `class SThree {` line, for a nested object closed on that line, and for a second definition sharing a body line with the first (`def c(): Int = 1; def d(): Int = 2`), in brace and indentation bodies alike. Each member is now indexed under its type with its own lines, and `read "file::sa"` prints just that definition, interpolated strings like `s"a${x}b"` included.
- **Kotlin members written on one line with their class are indexed.** In `class KTwo { fun ka(): Int { return 1 }; const val MAX = 3; fun kb() = 4 }` only the class was indexed, so `symbol ka` said "No matches". The same went for one-line interfaces and objects, for a method on the `class KThree {` line, for a companion object or nested class closed on that line, and for a second function sharing a body line with the first (`fun c() = 1; fun d() = 2`). Each member is now indexed under its type with its own lines, and `read "file::ka"` prints just that function, string templates like `"a${x}b"` included.
- **PHP members written on one line with their class are indexed.** In `class Single { public function alpha() { return 1; } private $x = 1; const K = 2; }` only the class was indexed, so `symbol alpha` said "No matches". The same went for a method on the `class Multi {` line, for one-line interfaces, traits and enums, and for a second property or method sharing a line with the first (`public $a = 1; public $b = 2;`). Each member is now indexed under its class with its own lines, and `read "file::alpha"` prints just that member rather than the whole class. Upgrading reindexes every file once.
- **A file with classic Mac line endings is indexed line by line.** A source file whose lines end in a lone carriage return (no line feed) was read as one long line: every TypeScript or Python function was indexed on line 1, a Scala object lost its methods, and a PHP class was not indexed at all, so `read "file::name"` printed the whole file. A lone carriage return now counts as a line break when a file is indexed and when a symbol's lines are cut back out of it, so each symbol gets its own lines and `read` prints only that symbol. Upgrading reindexes every file once.
- **Scala 3 `given` instances are indexed.** `given intOrd: Ord[Int] with`, the Scala 3.6 form `given intOrd: Ord[Int]:`, an alias like `given global: ExecutionContext = ForkJoinPool()` and an anonymous `given Ord[Int] with` defined nothing the index could find, so `symbol intOrd` said "No matches" and the methods in a given's body were listed under no type. A given with a body is now indexed as an object holding its methods, an alias or abstract given as a value, and an anonymous given under the name Scala gives it (`given_Ord_Int`). Upgrading reindexes Scala files once.
- **A nameless `export default` is indexed as `default`.** `export default function () {}`, `export default function* () {}`, `export default () => {}` and `export default class {}` defined nothing the index could find, so `symbol default` said "No matches" and `read "file::default"` said not found. TypeScript and JavaScript files and Vue, Svelte and Astro scripts now index each one as `default`, with its function or class kind, and an anonymous class's methods stay indexed under their own names. A Vue, Svelte or Astro script's `export default class extends Base {}` is no longer indexed as a class named `extends`. A default that re-exports a value (`export default foo`) or an options object (`export default {...}`) still defines no `default` symbol.
- **A nested member's symbol read keeps its first line's indent.** `read "file::create"`, `symbol` and `brief` printed a method's first line flush left over a body still indented to the class, because the stored text starts at the method's first character. The indent is now taken from the file, as long as that line still opens with the stored text, so the read matches a line-range read of the same lines.
- **A symbol read on a CRLF file ends its lines with LF, like a line-range read.** `read "file::symbol"`, `symbol` and `brief` printed a CRLF file's stored body with its carriage returns, while `read "file@1-3"` over the same lines printed LF, so the two reads of one function differed byte for byte. Every read surface now prints LF.
- **`image-meta` refuses an image whose header gives no width or height.** A file holding a GIF signature followed by a zeroed screen descriptor (named `.gif`, or mislabeled `.png`) printed `Dimensions: 0x0` and exited 0, so it read as a real, empty image. It now fails with `token-goat: <file> is not a readable image: its gif header gives no width or height`, the same wording a corrupt image already got, and exits 1.
- **`pdf-extract` and `pdf-locate` say when a `--pages` range runs past the last page.** `--pages 2-5` on a two-page PDF printed page 2 and said nothing about pages 3-5, while `--pages 9` on the same file was refused, so a range that ran off the end read as a complete answer for every page it asked for. Both commands now print `--pages 2-5 runs past the document's 2 pages; showing page 2.` on stderr, leaving stdout to the text or the `--json` body.
- **`xlsx-head` on a sheet with no cells says the sheet is empty.** It printed an empty fence, which reads as a failed or cut-off read, while `xlsx-sheets` already listed the same sheet as `(empty)`. It now prints `(nothing to show: the sheet is empty)`, the note `xlsx-range` gives for the same sheet. The text the embedding pipeline indexes for a workbook is unchanged.
- **`xlsx-range` stops at the sheet's used range and names what it left out.** Rows and columns past the last used cell printed as blank cells with nothing marking them, so `--range A1:E9` on a five-row, three-column sheet read like a sheet with empty records, and a range wholly below the data printed a column header over empty lines. The output now ends in a note such as `(not shown: rows 6-9 and columns D-E, past the sheet's used range A1:C5)`, and a range with nothing in it says so.
- **`read "file::symbol"` prints the symbol's doc comment above its body, and an `outline` doc hint ends in an ellipsis when the doc goes on.** The outline clipped a doc to its first line and marked nothing when later lines followed, so "Deletes the user." read as the whole doc while its second line warned that every backup goes too, and the read it points to for the full doc printed the body alone. The doc comment now comes back as its own source lines, and the header counts them (`# 3 lines + 4-line doc comment`).
- **`dead --kind` names an unrecognized kind in a `token-goat:` error with the kind escaped.** The message had no prefix and printed the caller's text as given.
- **`changed`, `diff` and `log` refuse a git ref that starts with `-` in a `token-goat:` error with the ref escaped.** The refusal had no prefix and printed the caller's ref as given, so a control character in it reached the terminal.
- **A failed git call reads as one clean `token-goat:` error.** `blame` and `log` ended their message in a literal `\n`, `diff` folded git's 130-line usage text into one escaped line, and `changed` outside a repository dumped those 131 lines raw with no `token-goat:` prefix. Every git failure now goes through one formatter: it trims git's output, stops at its usage text, and says `git <command> failed: not a git repository` in one line when there is no repository.
- **`recall --limit 0` and `search --limit 0` are refused with the same message the other `--limit` commands give.** An empty page answered neither: `recall` printed "Showing 0 entries; more are available" above "No cache entries match", and `search` printed "No results found across active channels []". Both now exit 1 with `--limit must be a positive number, got: "0"`, and a negative value gets the same wording instead of "non-negative".
- **`search` applies `semantic.max_distance` to its semantic channel and says when that channel's matches are weak, the way `semantic` does.** The channel took every hit the vector scan returned, so a floor you set narrowed `semantic` and left `search` unchanged, and a query with no real answer still listed a page of unrelated chunks with nothing to say they were noise. A `(note: semantic channel ...)` line now reports a floor that emptied the channel or a closest hit above `semantic.weak_distance`, and `--json` carries the weak case as `lowConfidence`. `semantic`'s own weak-match notice also escapes the query it quotes.
- **This repository's development tree is back to a clean `npm audit`, as [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md#dependency-advisories) says.** `proxy-addr`, reached through the MCP SDK's `express`, is resolved to the patched 2.0.8, and `source-map-js`, reached through `vite` and `postcss`, to the patched 1.2.2. Neither ever reached an installed copy of token-goat. No reindex is needed.
- **`html-outline`, `html-query --json` and `dep-docs` fence and redact what they print, and `html-query` redacts too.** A page's title, headings and element text, and an installed package's README, package.json fields and type declarations, are text someone else wrote. `html-outline` and `dep-docs` printed them bare, `html-query` fenced its printed form without redacting it and printed `--json` bare, so an instruction planted in the page or README reached the model as plain text and an access key in it was printed whole. The printed forms are now redacted and always fenced (`untrusted-html-content` for HTML, `untrusted-file-content` for `dep-docs`), and the `--json` forms redact every string and fence each one the injection scan flags, so the output stays JSON.
- **A long `pr-slice` diff, comment thread or description, and a long `html-query` result, keep the closing tag of their untrusted-content fence.** The output was fenced first and capped second, and the cap keeps only leading lines, so past `overflow_guard.max_tokens` it cut the closing tag off and left token-goat's own "output capped" line inside the fence. The cap now runs first and the fence wraps what it kept, with the cap line after the closing tag.
- **`csv-query`, `csv-profile`, `zip-list`, `zip-read`, `sqlite-query`, `sqlite-schema`, `sqlite-tables` and `describe` on a database fence and redact the file content they print.** These commands printed cells, archive entries, rows and table or column names bare, so an instruction planted in a CSV cell or a database row reached the model as plain text and a key in it was printed unredacted. They now print it the way `pdf-extract`, `xlsx-head` and `docx-text` already did: redacted, then fenced under `untrusted-file-content`, with `--json` output fencing each flagged value in place so it stays JSON. A `zip-read` long enough to hit the output cap keeps its closing tag, with the cap note after it. `json-query`, `yaml-query`, `xml-query` and `config-get` stay unfenced, like `read`, because they read the project's own files.
- **`config-get` on a YAML file or Markdown frontmatter prints the value, not the syntax around it.** A block value printed as `|` or `>-` instead of its text, an anchored value printed as `&x 5`, an alias as `*x`, a mapping or list as an empty line, and a key written in quotes was "not found", all with exit 0 where a value came back. YAML is now read by the same parser `yaml-query` uses: block text prints whole, anchors and aliases resolve, a mapping or list prints as JSON, a key with no value prints `null`, and a plain value keeps the spelling the file gives it, so `version: 2.0` still prints `2.0`. A file that is not valid YAML now exits 1 with a parse error.
- **A whole-file read piped through an output filter no longer draws the compress hint.** After `cat src/x.ts | head -200` printed more than 4 KB from a file with no multi-line comments to fold, the Bash hook suggested `token-goat compress -c "cat src/x.ts | head -200"`, which has nothing to save on a file read. Line-range reads such as `sed -n 1,120p f | cut -c1-200` were already exempt; the same read of a file whose comments could be folded stayed quiet only because the fold rewrote its output first.
- **`refs a,b` reports a call where no symbol has references as an error on stderr.** With every named symbol unreferenced, `refs a,b` exited 1 but printed `a: (no references found)` and `b: (no references found)` to stdout and nothing to stderr, so a script saw a failure with no error and the reason sitting where results go. Those lines now go to stderr under one `token-goat:` line, as `refs a` already reports its miss. A call where some symbols have references still lists the misses beside the hits on stdout and exits 0, and `--json` still prints its per-symbol map on stdout.
- **`uninstall --purge` prints every directory with one kind of separator, and each directory once.** A `TOKEN_GOAT_HOME` set with forward slashes was printed as spelled, beside a data directory printed with backslashes on Windows (`Nothing to purge at C:/x/home/.token-goat.` above `Purged C:\x\...\token-goat`). When `TOKEN_GOAT_HOME` named the data directory under a second spelling, the same directory was purged and then reported as "Nothing to purge". Both directories are now resolved to the platform's own spelling, and two spellings of one directory count once.
- **The pre-compact manifest and the re-read denies count only the reads since the last compaction.** A compaction drops earlier reads from context, but the manifest's "Superseded file reads" rows and the source-file re-read deny counted every read in the session. A file read twice before a compaction and once after it was denied as "Tried to read this file 3 times already" on its next read, and a file read once on each side of a compaction, or edited before it and read once after it, was listed as superseded with nothing in context to supersede. Each file entry now keeps a snapshot of its counts when its first read after a compaction lands, and the time of its last edit, both saved with the session, so the counts and the "edited after being read" reason cover the current context only.
- **Commands suggested by `symbol`, `semantic`, `doctor` and `install` are set in backticks.** `symbol`'s preview line ended `full body: token-goat read "src/a.ts::fn")`, `semantic` wrote `use: token-goat symbol "fn")`, and `doctor`, `install` and the embedding-status message wrapped commands in plain double or single quotes. Run through the check that hook output passes, each of those read on past the command to the `)` or `'` after it, and the command was replaced with "command omitted". They are now fenced like every hint, so they survive that check unchanged, and the `diff` and `log` help examples quote their revision range. The `json-query` command a `symbol` miss suggests for a nested JSON or YAML key now quotes the file path too, so a path holding a space stays one argument, and keeps a key holding `$` in single quotes so the shell leaves it alone.
- **A suggested command keeps a path that holds a space in one argument.** Hints and "Try:" lines wrote the path bare, so for a file such as `my proj/big file.ts` the suggested `token-goat scope my proj/big file.ts:1` failed with "too many arguments" and `token-goat skeleton my proj/...` with "Could not read: my". Every suggested path is now in double quotes, and `answer` quotes a path in its `via:` line when the path holds a space.
- **`outline`, `symbol` and `read` reparse a file an older token-goat indexed.** They reparsed a file only when its bytes had changed, so after an upgrade that changed what a language's parser extracts, an unchanged file kept the old spans. The session-start sweep that catches this runs only in a project set up with `token-goat index`, so a file these commands had indexed on demand anywhere else kept the old answer indefinitely. They now also reparse a file whose rows carry another parser version's stamp.
- **A one-line Scala definition no longer takes the next brace block as its body.** A `def b: Int = 2` or `val a = 1` followed by an unindexed brace construct, such as a `locally { ... }` block or a braced extension block, was recorded as running through that block, so `read "f.scala::b"` returned it too. A Scala `=` body now ends where its expression ends, as Kotlin's `fun f() = expr` already did; a braced body, a `match` block and a leading-dot continuation still span as before.
- **Scala 3 extension methods are indexed.** A method declared behind an `extension (c: Circle)` clause, whether on the clause's own line or in an indented or braced block below it, produced no symbol, so `symbol area` and `read "Shapes.scala::area"` found nothing. Each method is now indexed as a function, parented to the object or class the clause sits in.
- **`symbol --kind` names a kind no indexed symbol carries.** Kinds are matched exactly and stored lower-case, so `symbol --grep . --kind Method` printed only `No matches for '.'`, which reads as an empty project. It now adds `no indexed symbol has kind 'Method'` and suggests `method`, as `dead --kind` already did; a recognized kind the project simply has none of prints as before.
- **In bypass mode token-goat never adds a prompt; it either compresses or leaves the command alone.** A compressed command went out on Claude Code with no permission decision in bypassPermissions mode, and any rewrite could in auto mode, so a rule matched against the wrapper could prompt for a call the user had chosen never to be asked about; an approved image Read or Agent briefing in bypassPermissions mode could also step past a `--disallowedTools` rule no hook sees. Now auto mode leaves every call alone, and bypassPermissions mode rewrites and approves a call only when no rule source a hook cannot read could apply to it: a host that answers prompts itself (the Agent SDK, an editor), a `--disallowedTools`, `--settings` or other unrecognized flag on the `claude` command line, a skill, command or agent whose `disallowed-tools` names a pattern, or a `PermissionRequest` hook; otherwise the call runs untouched.

- **A token-goat hint no longer points Claude Code around a Read deny or ask rule.** A Read of a lock file or a `node_modules` file, a Grep for definitions in one file, or a `cat` of a file under a path such as `Read(./secrets/**)` was answered with a hint naming `token-goat json-query`, `read` or `skeleton`, which reads the file with no Read rule applying. On Claude Code and VS Code those hints are now held back wherever a Read deny or ask rule could cover the file, and Claude Code answers the call itself.
- **On Windows, a hook run through PowerShell no longer leaves its output held open by the hook server it starts.** PowerShell, which Copilot CLI runs hook commands through, hands the program it starts its own output as an extra inheritable handle beside the three standard ones. The native `tg-hook` client cleared only those three before running the Node command, so the hook server that command started in the background inherited the harness's output pipe and kept it open until the server exited, up to 30 minutes later. `tg-hook` now clears the inherit flag on every handle it holds. With the Node command alone (no native client), the first call that starts a server still leaves that output held, since Node cannot see the extra handle
- **On GitHub Copilot CLI, an image outside the working directory is no longer pointed at a shrunk copy.** Copilot asks before reading outside the working directory, the system temp folder and folders added with `/add-dir`, and the shrunk copy lives in the temp folder, so viewing it could skip the prompt the original image would have raised. Only an image inside the working directory is shrunk now, as on Claude Code.
- **On opencode, an image Read is no longer pointed at a shrunk copy.** opencode's `read` tool checks its `read` and outside-directory permissions against the path the plugin hands it, so a `read` deny or ask rule on the original image stopped applying once the path named a temp copy. The hook now passes on opencode, and the plugin no longer writes a copy even when the hook answers with one; run `token-goat install --opencode` again to replace an installed plugin.

- **On Copilot CLI, opencode and Grok, commands are no longer wrapped.** No hook can read the rules these harnesses check a command against: Copilot CLI's `--allow-tool` and `--deny-tool` arrive on its command line, opencode's can come from an organization account or a well-known URL and its check for directories outside the project reads the command text, and Grok applies a rewrite before its own policy and prompt see the call. A wrapped `cd ../outside && go vet ./...` no longer reached opencode's outside-directory prompt. On Copilot CLI and opencode, a recognized command's output is still compressed after it has run.

- **opencode is recognized as opencode.** token-goat looked for an `OPENCODE_SESSION_ID` or `OPENCODE_SESSION` variable that opencode never sets, so every opencode hook ran as an unknown harness, or as Claude Code, Codex or Gemini when an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY` or `GEMINI_API_KEY` was set; with an OpenAI key, opencode got Codex's payload mapping and its compaction never received the session manifest. opencode is now recognized by the `OPENCODE_PID` it sets, when that names the hook's own process or its parent, so another tool started from an opencode shell is not taken for it.

- **On Codex, a command a rules file names is no longer compressed.** Codex matches its `prefix_rule`s against a command's words, and `token-goat compress` hid them, so a `forbidden` or `prompt` rule for `curl` or `go vet` stopped applying and an `allow` rule stopped running the command outside the sandbox. token-goat now reads every Codex rules layer (the system folder, `~/.codex`, `CODEX_HOME`, each `.codex` above the current directory) and leaves a command alone when any rule could match it; a rules file it cannot parse, or an administrator's `requirements.toml` or managed preference that adds rules, leaves every command alone. Cloud requirements are not visible to a hook; see [security.md](docs/security.md).

- **A compressed command now honours the project's rules after the session's directory moves.** token-goat looked for Claude Code settings only from the command's current directory upward, but Claude Code reads them from the folder the session started in and, for local settings in a worktree, from the main checkout. So a deny rule there was missed once a command ran in another directory or in a linked worktree, and a `settings.local.json` allow in a subdirectory, which Claude Code never reads, got a command approved. Rules are now read from the session's starting folder (`CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`), its main checkout and the current directory, a command run outside the project is left uncompressed, and a local allow counts only in the folder Claude Code actually reads it from.

- **An allow rule in `.claude/settings.local.json` outside a git repository no longer gets a compressed command approved.** token-goat counts that file's allow rules only when git reports the file untracked, but it also read git's "not a repository" answer as untracked, so a folder unpacked from an archive that ships its own `settings.local.json` could have its rewritten commands approved by token-goat. The same answer comes from a repository git refuses to open under `safe.directory`. Only a file git reports untracked inside a repository counts now; otherwise a command that allow covers runs unwrapped and Claude Code applies its rules as usual.

- **A symlink, junction or 8.3 short name no longer carries a rewrite past a Read or Edit rule.** Claude Code checks a path rule against the file a path resolves to as well as the path itself, but token-goat only looked at the path as written, so an image read through a junction `pics` pointing at a denied `secrets` folder, or `go build -o pics/app ./...`, was rewritten, and an image read approved, where Claude Code would have refused the original. The real spelling of the path, and of every path a shell command names, is now checked too, and a command writing into a protected folder such as `.git` through a link is left alone.

- **Compressed shell commands no longer skip the auto-mode classifier.** In auto mode, and in plan mode, which runs the classifier by default, token-goat approved its rewrite of a read-only command or of one your allow rules covered, but auto mode drops broad allow rules such as `Bash(npm run *)` and reviews even read-only commands, so the approval let the call run unreviewed. Those modes now leave such a command unwrapped and send any other rewrite through with no decision. bypassPermissions no longer gets an approval either, since it runs the wrapper with no prompt anyway.

- **A file name holding a curly double quote can no longer slip a second command into a suggestion for PowerShell users.** PowerShell ends a double-quoted string at “, ” and „ as well as at a plain `"`, so a repository file named to close the quote, run a command and reopen it got through the check that strips broken suggestions from hints, which only looked at plain quotes. Those suggestions are now replaced with "command omitted" like any other broken one. Curly apostrophes and en dashes in a file name stay literal inside the quotes, so those files keep their suggestions.

- **Shrunk image copies no longer pile up in the system temp folder.** The smaller copy written for Claude Code, VS Code and Copilot CLI to read was only cleaned up when a later image was shrunk, so the last copies from each session stayed indefinitely. The background worker now deletes copies older than an hour on its hourly cleanup, and still never touches a copy young enough to be in use.

- **An image read through a shrunk copy now counts as a read of that image.** When token-goat answered a large image's Read with a smaller copy, the session never recorded the original, so the pre-compact manifest and the cross-session read list left it out even though the model had seen it. The session now records the image that was asked for, once, the same way it records a Read token-goat passes through, and never the temporary copy's path.

- **`image_shrink.ocr_enabled` is retired, since the Read hook no longer runs OCR for it to switch.** `config get` and `config set` now refuse the key and `TOKEN_GOAT_OCR_ENABLED` is no longer read. An existing config.toml or `.token-goat.toml` that still carries it loads as before, `config validate` does not flag it, and the next save drops it. `token-goat image-text` still reads `ocr_lang` and `ocr_min_confidence`.

- **Image shrinking no longer adds a megabyte of base64 text on Codex, Gemini CLI and other harnesses that cannot use it, and `stats` counts only shrinks that reached the model.** On those harnesses the hook could only put the shrunk image beside the Read as text, and the Read still loaded the original, so each screenshot cost its full image plus the text, while `stats` booked the difference as saved. The hook now leaves those reads alone. On opencode, OpenClaw, Copilot CLI and pi the host writes the copy itself, so no saving is booked there either; it is booked only on Claude Code and VS Code, where the Read is pointed at a copy token-goat wrote. The hook also no longer swaps a text-heavy screenshot for its OCR text, which sat beside the image rather than replacing it; `token-goat image-text` still extracts text on request.

- **Compressed commands under PowerShell no longer turn another tool's `-c` into a Python script.** On Windows, Codex and Copilot CLI commands that token-goat compresses run under PowerShell, where it moves an inline Python script from `python -c "..."` onto standard input so PowerShell cannot strip its quotes. The match took any program whose name ends in "py", and any `-c` after the interpreter, so `mypy -c "x: int = 1"` ran as `mypy -` and `python -m pytest -c setup.cfg` handed `setup.cfg` to Python as code. It now applies only to `python`, `python3` or `py` (with or without a path or `.exe`), and only when every word before `-c` is one of Python's own options.

- **token-goat no longer auto-approves the commands it rewrites, and your deny and ask rules apply to them.** To compress a command's output token-goat wraps it in `token-goat compress`, and on Claude Code it answered every such rewrite with an approval, so a command that would have asked you ran without asking, and a rule such as `Bash(curl:*)` in your deny list never matched, because Claude Code saw the wrapper rather than `curl`. A large image's shrunk copy slipped past a `Read(./private/**)` deny the same way. token-goat now reads your Claude Code settings first: where a deny or ask rule could apply, or a settings file cannot be read, it leaves the call as you wrote it, and it approves a rewrite only when Claude Code would have run the original without asking anyway.

- **`config set` no longer repeats a project path's token-goat marker as if token-goat said it.** When a project's `.token-goat.toml` overrides the key just saved, the warning names that file, and it printed the path and the validation reason raw. A project directory named to start with `[tg]` then put a line in token-goat's own voice into the warning, which reaches the model whenever the command runs as a tool. The path and reason now pass through the same escaping as the rest of the report. The check that looks for unescaped output now also watches the shared stderr writer, which is how this one was found; every other error it covers was already escaped.

- **`waste` and `audit` print a missing-transcript error as an error.** `waste --copilot --transcript` with a file that does not exist, `waste --copilot` with no Copilot session, and `waste` or `audit` with no session found for the project all wrote their message to standard output under a report heading such as `# token-goat waste (Copilot CLI)`, then exited 1, so the failure looked like the start of a report. Each now prints one `token-goat:` error line on standard error and no heading. With `--json` the error is still a JSON document on standard output, and `waste --transcript` with a missing file now gives that JSON document too instead of plain text.

- **`uninstall --purge` now exits with an error when nothing was purged.** When it refused because the background worker was running, or a data directory would not delete, it printed the error and still exited 0, so a script checking the exit code believed the data was gone. Both cases now exit 1.

- **The "use skeleton or outline" tip after reading a long source file now appears once.** It came back word for word on every read of the same file, so a file read in five slices carried five copies, and after a slice it still spoke of "a future full re-read" though nothing had been read in full. It now shows once per file until the conversation is compacted, and after a slice it says the commands replace paging through the file.

- **Editing one file in a project you never indexed no longer makes token-goat call it indexed.** The edit hook indexes the file you just changed, and session start took any symbols at all as proof the whole project was indexed: it told the model "this project is indexed" and then queued every other tracked file with the note "reindexing N files that changed outside this session", though nothing had changed. `install` skipped the project's first index for the same reason. token-goat now remembers which projects were indexed on purpose, by `token-goat index` or by `install`, and only those get the indexed reminder and the drift check. Projects indexed before this release keep both without re-indexing.

- **Reading a file in slices no longer marks it as safe to drop at compaction.** The note token-goat hands Claude Code before it compacts listed a file read as lines 1-300, then 301-600, then 601-900 under "Superseded file reads", telling the summarizer that only the latest copy was current, though each slice held different lines. The same happened to a file edited after only a slice of it was read. A read now counts as superseded only when a later whole-file read, or an edit after a whole-file read, replaces it, and subagent reads merged into the note keep their whole-file counts.

- **Deny messages keep their suggested commands.** When token-goat refused a whole-file read of a large or already-read file, the "to edit it anyway" advice was swallowed and the model saw `token-goat (command omitted: the path contains shell metacharacters)` instead of the `replace` and `write-file` commands, even for a plain path like `src/parser.ts`. The same happened to the PDF, Excel and PowerPoint read advice, the hint for a re-read tool spill file, and the XML hint for a shell XML parse. The safety check that hides commands a file path could break out of was tripping on token-goat's own `<base64>`-style placeholders, so those are now quoted and every suggestion in the source is checked against it.

- **Shrunk images now actually reach Claude Code.** Reading a large image on Claude Code handed the model the shrunk copy as base64 text beside the call, where Claude Code cut it to a 2,000-character preview, while the Read itself still loaded the full original; `token-goat stats` counted the difference as saved anyway (about 10 MB for one 12 MB photo). The Read is now pointed at a temporary file holding the shrunk copy, the way VS Code already worked, and the saving is counted only once that file is written. Only images inside the session's working directory are shrunk, because the rewritten read is approved by the hook and Claude Code asks before reading anything outside it.

- **Every command error now starts with `token-goat:`.** Most failing commands printed a bare message such as `Symbol 'foo' not found in 'nope.ts'` or `Could not read: nope.csv`, a few spelled the prefix themselves, and argument mistakes came out as commander's `error: unknown option`, so whether an error said where it came from depended on the command. They now all print one `token-goat:` line, followed by any suggestion lines as before. `bash-history`, `web-history`, `mcp-history`, `history`, `compact-doc`, `fetch-image` and `project` no longer print the same error twice. A `--json` error body is left as it was so it still parses, and on a failure the "extra argument ignored" note now comes after the error rather than before it. `bench` prints its report to stdout even when a check fails, since the report is the answer and the exit code grades it.

- **Files `index` leaves for the background worker are embedded even when the worker is already running.** Once `index` has spent its 20 seconds on embedding, or when only the worker may download the embedding model, it parses the remaining files and leaves their embedding to the worker. It never told a running worker about them, and a worker only looks for unembedded files once, when it starts, so they stayed out of `semantic` until it was next restarted: in a repository of 2,001 files, 1,448 were still unembedded minutes after `index` returned. `index` now puts those files on the worker's queue, and a running worker starts on them straight away.

- **`index` on a project in the system temp directory no longer leaves files unembedded for good.** After 20 seconds of embedding, `index` said the remaining files were "left for the background worker to embed", but the worker never indexes anything under the temp directory, so those files never got embedded and `semantic` kept missing them: in a project of 401 files, 259 were still unembedded minutes later with the worker running. `index` now embeds every file of such a project before it returns. When the embedding model is not downloaded and only the worker could fetch it, `index` now says the files were not embedded and how to download the model, instead of promising that the worker will do it.

- **Re-reading lines that changed on disk is no longer refused as already read.** Token-goat refuses a ranged `Read` of lines it has already shown in the session for as long as the file still holds what it held then. A change made outside the session's own Write and Edit calls, such as a formatter or `git checkout` run through Bash, another process, or your editor, went unnoticed once any other part of the file had been read since, so going back to the first lines was refused as "already read this session" although their new text had never been shown. The refusal also came back after a re-read of part of the same lines had already noticed the change, because saving the session restored the line ranges that re-read had just dropped. A change on disk now clears the file's recorded line ranges before the next one is recorded, and the clearing survives the save.

- **A `json-query` or `yaml-query` path with a space before `|` now reads the key in front of it.** The pipe is documented with spaces around it, `members[] | {id, name}`, but when the part before it ended in a plain key, the space became part of that key's name: `org | {name, tier}` failed with `key 'org ' does not exist`, and `..address | city` printed an empty result with exit status 0 instead of every city. `mcp-output --json-query` did the same. Whitespace after a key name is now ignored, as whitespace before one already was, and a key that really ends in a space is still reached with a quoted segment, `["org "]`.

- **`bash-output`, `web-output` and `mcp-output` line numbers and ranges now match the text they read.** A blank line that ended a `--lines` or `--grep` result was left out, so `--lines 1-2 -n` on a file whose second line is blank printed only line 1, and `--grep '^$'` could report a blank line past the end of the file. A private key in a file read with `--file` was hidden as a single line, so every `-n` number after it was off and `--lines` fetched the wrong lines; each line of the key is now hidden in place, so the numbering stays the file's own and no key material is shown.

- **A `json-query`, `yaml-query` or `mcp-output --json-query` path that matches nothing now says so and exits 1.** A path that fanned out with `[*]`, a filter or `..key` and then kept nothing, such as `items[*].missing`, `items[status=archived]` or `[*]` over an empty array, printed one empty line with exit status 0, which looked like an answer; a missing key on a plain path already failed with a message. `items[*].name | [0]` did the same, because after a fan-out the `[0]` applies to each name rather than to the list of names. These now exit 1 with a message naming the step that came up empty and what it found there, and for a trailing index it suggests `--head 1`; `--json` still prints the empty result envelope. The message names what the path reached in words that agree with the count, such as "both values it reached are objects" or "the 4 values it reached are an object, 2 numbers and a string". A value that really is an empty array or object still prints as `[]` or `{}`.

- **`search` shows the matched text for a match far along a long line.** A text match's preview was the opening of its line, so when the match sat past the first 120 or so characters, as in a long array literal or a wide Markdown table row, the printed result did not contain what was searched for. The preview is now a window of the line around the match. A one-line result that also matched a longer passage by meaning now prints the matching line too, rather than the opening of that passage.

- **A `sed -n`, `head` or `Get-Content -TotalCount` re-read of a file that changed on disk is no longer told it overlaps lines already read.** A shell read of lines the session had already been shown was answered with "You already read lines ... overlaps" even after a formatter, a `git checkout`, another process or your editor had changed the file, so the new text was waved off as something to recall from earlier output. The `Read` tool already noticed such a change; shell reads did not, and a file read only through `sed` or `head` had nothing on record to notice it with. A change on disk now clears the file's recorded line ranges before a shell read is measured against them, and the hint returns only while the lines still hold what was shown.

- **The note under a query cut short by `--head` now says "1 more item", not "1 more items".** When `--head` hid exactly one match, `json-query`, `yaml-query`, `xml-query`, `html-query`, `csv-query`, `xlsx-query` and `mcp-output --json-query` reported "1 more items elided" (or "1 more elements", "1 more rows"). The count and its noun now agree.

- **A `json-query`, `yaml-query` or `mcp-output --json-query` search that hit its traversal limit before finding anything now says so.** On a deeply nested or very large document, a `..key` search that stopped at the limit with no matches printed only "these are not necessarily all the matches" with nothing above it. It now says no matches were found before the search stopped, and that the unsearched part may still hold some. The exit code stays 0, since a search cut short is not a definite miss.
- **`json-query`, `yaml-query` and `xml-query` errors now start with `token-goat:` like other command failures.** A missing key, a fan-out that matched nothing, an unreadable or unparseable file, or a bad `--head` printed a bare message on stderr, so it read as if another program had failed. These errors now go through the same printer as other command failures.
- **The `stats.record_zero_savings` config key is gone, because nothing read it.** `config set` accepted it and `config get` reported it, but setting it changed nothing: it was carried over from the Python version, where it filtered zero-saving stat rows, and the current stats count those events on purpose. A config.toml that still holds `[stats] record_zero_savings = ...` (every save before sparse saving wrote it) loads as before, `config validate` does not flag it, and the next save drops it.
- **`config validate` reports a misspelled key in a project `.token-goat.toml` as unknown, with a suggestion.** A project file holding `[foo] bar = 1` or `[hints] quiet_hour = "x"` was reported as `[project_value_ignored] foo.bar (1 is not usable; in effect: undefined)`, which does not say the key does not exist. These now read `[project_unknown_section] foo` and `[project_unknown_key] hints.quiet_hour (did you mean: quiet_hours?)`, found the same way as an unknown key in the global `config.toml`, and a retired key is still passed over.
- **Notes that count what was left out now say "1 more item", not "1 more items".** When exactly one thing was hidden, the compressed `json` array view printed `[... 1 more items not shown]`, and the same "1 more lines", "1 more commits", "1 more rows" or "1 more files" wording appeared in other compressed command output, `refs --top`, `xlsx-head`, `trace`, folded reads and the project notes block. Each of them now uses the singular for one. Because the `xlsx-head` note lives in the spreadsheet reader, upgrading re-embeds already-indexed `.xlsx` files once; no other file type is affected.

## [2.9.30] - 2026-10-03

Upgrading reparses the index. The parser stamp moves for every language, so each file is read once more. CSS, SCSS, Less, Vue, Svelte and Astro files come back with their full rule and declaration spans, and minified bundles already in the index are removed in the same pass. Every indexed file is also re-embedded once, because the embedding stamp moved with the check that stops a slow embedding run from overwriting newer vectors; Markdown files that open with a front-matter block come back with corrected heading boundaries. The heading-matcher and spreadsheet-redaction fixes below move the parser, Markdown and Excel stamps too, and the same single pass covers them.

### Added

- **User-scope Copilot CLI installation synchronizes existing project instructions.** Running `token-goat install` in user scope updates personal instructions and also refreshes any `.github/copilot-instructions.md` file present in the current workspace. This keeps project-level routing blocks current without requiring a separate `--local` install run.
- **Guidance blocks list static validation and path separation failure shapes.** The generated instructions tell agents to run fast syntax checks such as `tsc --noEmit`, `node --check`, or `ruff check` before running test suites, and to ensure arguments in multi-path shell commands remain properly separated.
- **Git post-merge and post-rewrite hooks rebuild the bundle and restart daemons.** Checking out a branch or pulling commits that update source files could leave the running agent executing against an outdated bundle or obsolete resident daemon. The new post-merge hook in `lefthook.yml` runs `scripts/post-merge.mjs` to rebuild the bundle with esbuild, restart resident hook servers and workers, refresh tool shims, and trigger a background reindex.
- **MCP tools work out which project you mean when `projectRoot` is left out.** A call such as `read` or `symbol` used to search the MCP server's own working directory, so a file in another project came back as not found. The server now takes the project from an absolute path when the file exists, then from the indexed file paths that end with the one you gave, then, for a bare name, from the project that defines that symbol. A project is only inferred if token-goat has indexed it and it sits inside the server's allowed roots. Otherwise the server keeps its own directory, so with `mcp.confine_reads_to_project_root` on, a path outside it is still refused. Passing `projectRoot` still wins.
- **C# files have references, so `refs --callers` and `brief` find C# callers.** The C# adapter extracted declarations but no call sites, so asking who calls a C# method returned nothing even when the project called it everywhere. Method calls and object creations are now recorded as references, with keywords, built-in type names and LINQ query words filtered out.
- **`json-query` and `yaml-query` support JMESPath and jq-style multi-field projections.** Querying structured data previously required pulling a single field at a time or dropping into inline shell scripts that broke on nested quotes. Queries now accept list projections like `items[*].[id, name]` and object projections like `items[*].{id, title: name}`. They support nested dot paths, array indices, empty-bracket iteration (`[]`), and pipe syntax (`|`). Missing fields evaluate to null, and projecting an array without an explicit wildcard automatically maps over each element.
- **`token-goat doctor --fix` and `token-goat install` automatically repair stale or missing harness hooks.** When updating token-goat or switching harness configurations across Claude Code, Copilot CLI, or other agents, wrapper scripts or hook registrations could point to outdated shims or missing binaries. The repair pass inspects active harness hook files, refreshes stale shim scripts, and restores broken hook registrations without requiring manual uninstalls or separate setup commands.
- **`doctor` checks that an instruction file carries token-goat's routing gate, and `doctor --fix` adds one when none is active.** Without the gate, agents treat token-goat's hints as optional and read whole files anyway. The check passes when `token-goat install` has written the gate into a user-level file (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` or Copilot CLI's `copilot-instructions.md`), or when the project's `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` or `.github/copilot-instructions.md` has one. `--fix` writes the gate into the project's instruction files only when neither is true, and creates `CLAUDE.md` if the project has none. A user-level gate already reaches every project, and a copy in a file you commit would be loaded twice every session, with nothing to update or remove it.
- **`doctor` warns when a project has only IDE workspace folders and no CLI agent is set up.** IDE hooks cannot trim the IDE's own built-in reads, so a `.vscode`, `.idea`, `.cursor` or `.vs` folder with no Claude Code, Codex or Copilot CLI setup gets a pointer to `token-goat install`. A CLI harness counts whether it is configured in the project or at user level by `token-goat install`.
- **`token-goat stats` flags low use of the surgical commands.** When at least 10 hints have fired and the commands they point to made up under a quarter of the total, the insights section shows the percentage and suggests `token-goat doctor --fix` to add the instruction gate.
- **Update checks work behind a company npm registry, are cached for a day, and show up where you already look.** `token-goat upgrade --check` asks `npm view` first, so a configured registry such as Artifactory or Nexus, its auth token and its CA certificate are used. It falls back to a direct request to the registry in `npm_config_registry` or to npmjs.org. A successful result is cached for 24 hours. When a newer version is out, `token-goat stats` shows it in its insights, the context added at session start mentions it, and step 7 of `doctor --fix` installs it.
- **The README explains what a whole-file read costs through the prompt cache.** A new section and a row in the comparison table cover cache write and read pricing and how a large read is paid for again on every later turn until the session compacts.
- **A project note can be anchored to a symbol, and it says so once that symbol changes.** A note records something true about the code when it was written, and nothing told the agent when the code moved on: a note about how a function behaves kept arriving at every session start after the function was rewritten or deleted. `token-goat note set <key> "<finding>" --anchor src/auth.ts::login` now records the symbol's body hash beside the note, and a note whose text names a `file::symbol` the index knows is anchored to it without the flag. Once the worker's reindex sees the body change, the note reads "(changed since note)" at session start, in `note list` and in `stats --payloads`; once the symbol is gone it reads "(anchored symbol gone)". The check follows the index rather than the file, so an edit the worker has not reached yet flags nothing, and a file the index has never seen flags nothing either. An explicit `--anchor` that names no indexed symbol is refused and the note is not set. The anchor is a comment line in the notes file, so an older token-goat still reads the note, though it drops the anchor the next time it saves the file. The compaction manifest shows notes without the marker: it is built synchronously, and on Claude Code the session start that follows a compaction brings the notes back with it. Loading the index reader only when a session starts keeps it off every other hook's startup; the markers still added 3 KB to what each hook loads, and the size guard's ceiling moved with them.
- **On Windows, Claude Code's Bash commands with two backslashes in a row are refused before they run.** Claude Code hands a Bash command to Git Bash as a command-line argument, and on the way every run of two or more backslashes is cut roughly in half. A lone backslash arrives intact. So an escaped Windows path, a regular expression or a `printf` format string runs as different text from what was written, and nothing reports an error: the command succeeds and does the wrong thing (reported upstream as anthropics/claude-code#85856). The refusal says why and what to do instead: write the text to a file with the Write tool and run it by path, use the PowerShell tool, which passes the command through unchanged, or use forward slashes in paths. It applies only to Claude Code on Windows. Other harnesses, and every other platform, never see it. Set `deny_bash_double_backslash = false` under `[hints]`, or `TOKEN_GOAT_DENY_BASH_DOUBLE_BACKSLASH=0`, to turn it off.
- **`token-goat stats --payloads` shows what token-goat adds to every session's context.** token-goat puts text into each session on its own: the routing reminder, the note of files that changed outside the session, and the project notes at session start, plus the block it installs into CLAUDE.md and the token-goat skill's description. None of that was visible anywhere, so its cost was a guess. The new flag prints each block with its size in characters and estimated tokens, the total a session start costs in this project, and the cap on the compaction manifest. It measures the same text the hook sends: in a test project, the session-start total it reported matched the length of the real hook's output exactly, both before and after indexing. For every note that names files, it also says how many of those files have been read since the note was set, which is a rough sign of whether the note still points at live code. `--json` prints the same report as JSON.
- **`token-goat install` indexes the project you run it in.** Before, a fresh install indexed nothing: session start only refreshes a project that already has symbols, so `symbol`, `read` and `semantic` came back empty until you found `token-goat index` and ran it yourself. Now, inside a git project with no index yet, `install` puts its tracked files on the worker's queue, wakes the worker, and prints one line with the file count. It does not parse anything itself, so install still returns in about the time `git ls-files` takes. In a test run on a fresh project, a function in it resolved through `token-goat symbol` about a second after install finished. `--no-index` or `TOKEN_GOAT_INSTALL_INDEX=0` turns this off. It is skipped, with a line saying why, for a folder that is not a git repository (which names `token-goat index --walk`), a project under `worker.blocked_roots`, a project under the OS temp folder, and a marker at or above the home folder, which would otherwise make every folder you own one project. A project that is already indexed is left to session start, and install says nothing about it.

- **`token-goat answer` can say what imports a file.** Ask "what imports src/stats.ts", "which files import it", "importers of it" or "what depends on it" and `answer` routes to the new `token-goat deps <file> --importers`, which lists the project files that import that one directly. Before, `answer` refused "what imports X" as a question it had no route for, and refused "what depends on" a file because that route only took symbols. The list is built from git-tracked files, so a file git does not track is refused with a line saying so rather than reported as imported by nothing. Asked about a symbol, `answer` says importers are per file and names the `deps` command for the file that defines it.
- **`token-goat listing-size` prices the skill and agent listings Claude Code puts in context.** Claude Code sends the name and description of every skill and every subagent type at the start of a session and again after each compaction, so a long description is paid on every re-send, whether or not the skill or agent is ever used. `skill-size` only prices skill bodies, which load when a skill runs. The new command reads the session transcript, which records what the model actually received, including plugin and nested-directory skills. It reports how large each listing is, how many times it was sent and how many bytes that came to, and ranks the largest descriptions. Pass a session id or transcript path, or nothing for the current project; `--top N` limits the ranking and `--json` gives the full report. On the session this was built in, the agent listing went out 73 times and came to 705 KB, more than the skill listing's 126 KB.
- **An opt-in refusal for Claude Code agent spawns that omit `subagent_type`.** Such a spawn runs as general-purpose, and its context starts with every tool and MCP schema on the machine. With `agent_scoped_spawn_deny = true` under `[hints]` (or `TOKEN_GOAT_AGENT_SCOPED_SPAWN_DENY=1`), the first one in a session is refused when tools-restricted agent definitions exist in `~/.claude/agents` or the project's `.claude/agents`, and the refusal names up to three of them. Re-issuing the same call runs it, since the refusal happens once per session. A spawn that names a `subagent_type` is never refused, `general-purpose` included. It is off by default because the hook cannot tell whether the task fits a narrower agent. `stats` counts each refusal as `agent_scoped_spawn_deny`, with no tokens credited.
- **`token-goat install --copilot` registers token-goat's MCP server with Copilot CLI.** It writes a `token-goat` entry to `~/.copilot/mcp-config.json` (or `$COPILOT_HOME/mcp-config.json`) in the shape `copilot mcp add` writes, so `copilot mcp list` shows it, and `uninstall --copilot` removes it while keeping every other server. Before, the README asked you to write that entry by hand. An entry written from those old instructions is replaced; any other `token-goat` entry is left alone and install stops with a message. A `--local` install does not touch the file, since Copilot reads it only at user scope.
- **`token-goat install --antigravity` wires token-goat into Google's Antigravity CLI (`agy`).** It adds a plugin folder at `~/.gemini/config/plugins/token-goat/` with hooks for agy's `PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse` events, and translates agy's own tool and argument names (`view_file` with `AbsolutePath`, `run_command` with `CommandLine`, and so on) so repeat-read hints and denials, bash output handling and post-edit indexing apply to agy sessions. A call token-goat lets through gets `{}` rather than `"allow"`, because in agy `"allow"` skips the approval prompt. `~/.gemini/config/hooks.json`, which other tools write to, is never edited, and `token-goat uninstall --antigravity` removes the plugin folder and any empty `~/.gemini` folders the install made. `install --all` includes it.
- **Hook calls on Windows and Linux run through a native client, `tg-hook`, instead of starting Node.** `install` now puts the binary in front of every hook command it writes, for Claude Code, Codex, Grok, Kimi and Copilot CLI, on Windows and Linux (x64 and arm64). `tg-hook` hands the call to token-goat's resident hook server and prints its answer; when the server cannot answer (off, not started yet, busy), it runs the ordinary Node hook command it wraps, with the same input, so no hook depends on the server being there. macOS keeps the Node command. Measured on one Windows machine with `scripts/bench-native-hook.mjs`, a served call took 11.6 ms at minimum and 14.5 ms at the median, against 43.5 ms and 47.1 ms for the Node hook script through the same server. To keep the Node form, set `native = "off"` under `[hooks]` in the global config or `TOKEN_GOAT_NATIVE_HOOKS=0` (the variable wins) and run `install` again; a project's `.token-goat.toml` cannot set this key. Setup and details: [Native hook client](docs/install.md#what-gets-installed).
- **`doctor` warns when Claude Code hooks that were running are gone.** If Claude Code ran token-goat hooks in the last seven days but neither `~/.claude/settings.json` nor the project's `.claude/settings.json` wires any now, and no `token-goat uninstall` was recorded since, `doctor` prints a `Claude Code hooks` warning with how many ran, when the last one did, and the command that puts them back. Before, a settings file that was overwritten or deleted left `doctor` silent, and the hooks stayed off until someone noticed the savings stop. `uninstall` now records its removal, so a deliberate one does not trigger the warning.
- **Claude Code shows, once, how much token-goat has saved.** The first Claude Code session start after token-goat has saved anything shows a one-line note: "token-goat has saved about N tokens so far. Run `token-goat stats` for the detail." N is the same all-time figure `stats` reports. The note uses Claude Code's `systemMessage` field, which you see and the model does not, so it costs no tokens. It is shown once per data folder and never again, and `doctor` has a `Savings receipt` row saying whether it was shown and on what date. Other harnesses do not get it, and a session start in one of them does not use up the one showing.
- **A weekly adoption table for maintainers.** `npm run adoption` prints npm downloads, GitHub stars and GitHub forks for each week since the repository was created, newest week first, and a scheduled workflow puts the same table in its job summary every Monday.
