It rebuilds the numbers from npm's and GitHub's public APIs each time, so nothing is stored or committed. It needs a GitHub token in `GH_TOKEN`, because GitHub does not list stargazers without one; `GH_TOKEN=$(gh auth token) npm run adoption` works locally. `--json` gives the rows as data and `--since YYYY-MM-DD` starts later. Stars and forks count only the ones that still exist, and npm downloads include CI installs, so the table shows a trend, not a head count. This is a repository script and does not ship in the package.
- **`doctor` reports the native hook client per harness.** Each `Native hooks` row says which form is wired (native, Node or mixed), whether the binary passes its self-test, and how many hook calls over the last seven days the server answered, how many fell back to Node and why, and how many ran the Node command directly. `doctor`'s own probe is left out of those counts. An entry whose binary is gone is reported as broken, since those events fail rather than fall back, and `install` rewrites it.
- **On Windows, hooks run a copy of `tg-hook.exe` kept in token-goat's data directory, not the one inside the npm package.** Windows cannot replace a running binary, and with hooks running the copy, an `npm install -g token-goat@latest` during a session can replace the packaged one even while a hook call is running. The copy sits under `native\<id>\` in the data directory; `install` refreshes it and moves a copy that is still running aside for a later `install` to remove. If the new copy cannot take its place, for instance because an antivirus is still scanning it, `install` retries for a moment and then leaves the old copy where it was or puts it back, whichever step the scanner blocked, so hooks already wired to it keep a program to run and the install writes the Node command instead. `uninstall` leaves it with the rest of token-goat's data, because a project-scope install elsewhere may still point at it.
- **`token-goat stats` counts answers served from an out-of-date index.** When `read`, `symbol`, `outline`, `skeleton`, `brief`, `refs`, `semantic`, `ask` or `trace --bodies` answers from rows for a file that changed on disk or no longer exists, the ledger records it under `stale_served:stale` or `stale_served:deleted`, with the command's name, once per answer. `stats` prints a `Stale answers:` line with the changed and deleted counts when there are any, and `stats --full` and `stats --json` list both kinds. Until now the only trace was the warning in the reply itself.
- **`token-goat stats` counts `answer` calls and how each one ended.** Every call books one row: `answer:<command>` for the command its `via:` line names (`symbol`, `callers`, `impact`, `test-for`, `exports`, `imports` or `deps`), with `answered` or the command's exit code, or `answer:refused` with the reason (`empty`, `judgement`, `no-intent`, `unresolved`, `ambiguous`, `symbol-only` or `file-needs-symbol`). The question itself is not stored. `stats --full` lists an `answer` row under By Command, and `stats --json` has the per-route split. Before this, a routed answer was booked only as the command it delegated to, and a refusal left no trace.
- **Semantic search matches on meaning in a default install, with nothing extra to install.** Embeddings run on the WebAssembly build of ONNX Runtime (onnxruntime-web 1.30.0), whose JavaScript is now bundled into token-goat. Its 14 MB binary, `ort-wasm-simd-threaded.wasm`, is downloaded once, the first time something is embedded: token-goat fetches the onnxruntime-web 1.30.0 tarball from registry.npmjs.org, checks it against a recorded SHA-256 and size, keeps only that file, checks it against its own recorded SHA-256 and size, and deletes the tarball. Nothing is installed and no install script runs. The kept copy is checked again every time it loads. With `network.offline` set the download is refused, and a copy placed by hand in the runtime folder is still accepted. `TOKEN_GOAT_MODEL_CACHE_DIR` shares the binary between data roots the way it already shared the model, and `npm run model:warm` fetches both. The native `onnxruntime-node` is still used whenever it is installed; it stays optional because it is about 288 MB installed. The published package grows by about 33 KB.
- **`doctor` and `semantic --preflight` say which runtime embeds.** `doctor`'s Embeddings row names the native runtime and its version, or the bundled WebAssembly runtime and whether its binary has been downloaded yet. `semantic --preflight` prints the active runtime and, for the WebAssembly one, whether the binary is downloaded; `--json` carries `runtime` and `runtimeBinaryPresent`. With the binary not yet downloaded and offline mode on, preflight reports the runtime missing and names the folder to copy the file into. When the WebAssembly runtime fails to start (a Node run with `--jitless`, or one without WebAssembly SIMD), the failure is recorded beside its binary, so a later `doctor` or `semantic --preflight` reports the runtime unavailable with the reason, where before only the process that tried knew and both called it ready. The record lapses when Node, its flags or token-goat change, after a day, or when it is deleted, and the first run under the same Node and flags that does start the runtime removes it. `token-goat capabilities` lists the new download as `network.embedding_runtime_download`.
- **VS Code chat's multi-file edit, web fetch, subagent and terminal-output tools reach token-goat's hooks.** VS Code 1.137 calls these tools `multi_replace_string_in_file`, `apply_patch`, `fetch_webpage`, `runSubagent` and `get_terminal_output`, and token-goat had no handler under those names. Every file a multi-file edit or a patch changes is now queued for reindexing, so the index no longer goes stale for them. A `fetch_webpage` call is checked against the web fetch deny and allow lists and the cloud metadata block for each URL it names. A `runSubagent` call gets the same session briefing as Claude Code's `Task`, with the project map taken from the open workspace folder, or left out when no folder is open.
- **A subagent's session briefing reaches the subagent itself in Copilot CLI and VS Code chat, not the parent's history.** token-goat now wires the hook each of them fires when a subagent starts (`subagentStart` in Copilot CLI, `SubagentStart` in VS Code) and answers it with the briefing. Before, the briefing was added to the prompt of the parent's `task` or `runSubagent` call, so every spawn left about 450 tokens of it in the parent conversation, sent again with each later request. Run `token-goat install --copilot` (and `install --vscode` if you use VS Code chat) again to add the new hook; until then the briefing keeps arriving the old way. The project map in the briefing, and in `token-goat map`, now escapes the project folder name and recent file names the same way it already escaped symbol names.
- **A long subagent report is compacted before the parent sees it in Copilot CLI.** Copilot's subagent-stop hook carries the subagent's answer and accepts a replacement for it, which then stands in for the answer in the parent's task result. token-goat now answers that hook with the compacted report (long code blocks trimmed to their ends, repeated blocks folded) and a pointer to the full copy, which `token-goat mcp-output <id> --full` prints. Before, the shim dropped the answer, so the report reached the parent in full. A report that is long only because it is long prose passes unchanged, as before.
- **`token-goat statusline` works as Copilot CLI's status line.** Copilot CLI sends a status line command the same session data Claude Code does, and the line showed the project and model correctly, but its context figure was not the one Copilot shows: Copilot's `used_percentage` is measured against a larger window and reads 0 until the first reply. The line now uses `current_context_used_percentage`, the figure Copilot's own footer shows, when the payload has it, and Claude Code's line is unchanged. [docs/install.md](docs/install.md#copilot-cli-users) shows the `~/.copilot/settings.json` setting.
- **`token-goat stats` records whether a refused whole-file re-read was of an unchanged file.** The "already read this session" denies decide from how many times a file was read, not from what it holds now. Each one now books a `reread_deny_identity` row naming the deny that fired and whether the file was identical to or changed from the session's last read. The check uses the snapshot kept from that read when there is one, and otherwise the file's size and modification time. A file with a snapshot that has not changed is already answered earlier with "unchanged since last read", so these denies mostly refuse files that did change.
- **`token-goat stats` counts edits made after a Read that showed only part of the file.** When a file's last Read in the session was an outline, a skeleton, a folded delivery, a harness-truncated one or a window that stopped short of the file, the next Edit of it books an `edit_after_fold` row naming that shape and the editing tool. A Read that delivers the whole file clears the record, and so does a Write, which supplies every line itself. A subagent's reads are kept apart from its parent's. `stats --json` lists the count.
- **`token-goat doctor --probe <harness>` checks that token-goat's context reaches the model.** `doctor` can confirm a hook is wired and ran, but not whether the harness passed what it said on to the model. The probe runs one real prompt through `claude`, `codex` or `copilot` headless (it costs one prompt on your own account), with a random marker the session-start and prompt hooks add to their context, then checks which markers the model repeats back. Each event reports one of four results: it reached the model; the hook ran but the model never saw its output; the hook did not run; or the event is not wired for that harness. Codex has no session-start hook, so its first row reads "not wired". Copilot runs without `--allow-all-tools`, because the prompt needs no tool. `--json` prints the report as JSON, and the command exits non-zero unless every wired event reached the model. The probe's own hook calls stay out of `stats`, and the marker files it leaves are deleted when it finishes.
- **`json-query`, `yaml-query` and `xml-query` read the document from stdin when the file argument is `-`.** Output from another command can be piped straight in without a temporary file. If nothing is piped in, or the pipe carries an empty document, the command says so instead of waiting or reporting a parse error.
- **`token-goat answer "what does X do"` answers through `brief`.** When X has exactly one definition in the project, "what does X do" and "what is X for" now print its body and callers instead of refusing. A name with several definitions still refuses, and the message says how many there are and where.
- **`json-query` and `yaml-query` take JMESPath and jq style projections.** `items[*].[name, id]` returns several fields per element, and `items[*].{name, id}` or `{n: name, owner: meta.owner}` builds an object with optional aliases. Projections nest, chain with `|`, and accept `[]` to iterate, and a missing field comes back as `null`, so a payload can be shaped without an inline script.
- **`doctor --repair` and `install` fix stale hooks in every installed harness.** After an update, an outdated hook shim, a missing hook event or an old command entry is rewritten in place, so a harness that stopped calling token-goat starts again without manual rewiring. A harness that is already wired is left alone, so neither command reports a repair it did not make. `doctor --repair` also queues the project for reindexing when its index was built by an older parser.
- **`bash-output`, `web-output`, `mcp-output` and `retrieve` can return an exact line range.** `--lines 120-180` prints those lines with nothing cut, `-n` puts the line number in front of each line, and `--context 3` shows three lines either side of every `--grep` hit, with `--` between groups. When the default view does cut lines, the marker now says which ones are missing and gives the `--lines` range that brings them back.
- **You can move the line where `semantic` calls its closest match weak, and see where your own queries land.** The line was fixed at 0.85, a figure measured on token-goat's own index. It is now `semantic.weak_distance`, held to 0.05–1.2 like `semantic.max_distance`, and a project's `.token-goat.toml` cannot set it, because raising it would make every poor hit read as confident. Each semantic query now records two numbers: its closest distance and, when the relevance floor turned results away, the nearest one it turned away. No query text is kept, and the rows expire with the rest of the stats. `semantic --distances` prints their percentiles and band counts for the current project, or for every project with `--all`, next to both thresholds. With fewer than 20 queries recorded it says so instead of judging a threshold.
- **The retrieval eval measures when an arm should have answered nothing.** `npm run eval:retrieval` now includes 15 questions this repository cannot answer, about cake recipes, Kubernetes ingress and the like. Each arm reports how often it abstains on those against how often it abstains on answerable questions, with a bootstrap interval on both. Two new lines catch failures that hit rate misses. The collision rate is the share of questions whose wrong first answer is the same file another question got. The hub line lists any file that appears in the top results for at least a quarter of all questions. For `semantic`, the report counts closest distances by band, fits a weak-match line on the training split and scores it next to the configured value on the test split. hit@k and MRR still count answerable questions only.

### Changed

- **`index` on a large repository returns in about 20 seconds of embedding instead of hours.** Embedding runs at roughly ten chunks a second on the bundled WebAssembly runtime, and `index` waited for every file, so a repository of a couple of thousand files kept it running for hours. It now embeds until 20 seconds have gone, not counting the first file, which loads the model. The rest are parsed and resolve by symbol straight away, and the background worker embeds them. `index` says how many it left. `index --embed` waits for all of them.
- **The skill `token-goat install` writes is now version 1.9.3, generated from a readable source.** The skill text shipped only as a compressed blob in `src/canonical_skill.ts`, so the one copy anyone could edit was the installed `SKILL.md`, and every install overwrote edits made there with the older text. Its source is now `src/canonical_skill.md`; `npm run skill:canonical` rewrites the blob from it and `npm run skill:canonical:check` fails when the two disagree. The installed skill picks up the Related Skills edits made to an installed copy (a reworded `superman` entry and a new `routing-agent-memory` entry), drops its inline changelog section, and opens with a comment saying where to edit it.
- **`search` ranks changelogs, archives and docs below the code they mention.** `semantic` already did this, but `search` gave every path the same weight, so a CHANGELOG line naming a function three times could sit above the function itself. `search` now applies the weights `semantic` uses: `semantic.archive_weight` for archive, plans, CHANGELOG and backup paths, `semantic.docs_weight` for docs and markdown. The weight is applied before `--limit`, so a down-weighted hit can fall off a short list instead of only moving down it.
- **`search` prints about 10% less.** Each result is now its rank, its location, the symbol when there is one, and the channels that found it, with the preview on the next line. The banner, the blank line between results, the bracket around each channel and the four-decimal fusion score are gone. The score only set the order, which the list already shows, and it is still in `--json`. On the retrieval eval's 57 queries the text output shrank by 9 to 11% on every search mode, and the JSON output is unchanged.
- **`dotnet test` output is shorter.** The test filter now drops the MSBuild and restore chatter, the test-host banner and copyright lines, and xUnit's discovery and start/finish lines, and it counts xUnit's `[PASS]`, `√` and `✓` result lines as passes. Failures, their stack traces and the summary are kept. In shells where token-goat cannot rewrite the command first, such as VS Code's terminal tool, `dotnet test` and `cargo test` are now compressed after they run, as `git diff` already was, and a repeat run still points at the stored copy of the first. Other commands in those shells are cached as before rather than rewritten.
- **A denied re-read says where the earlier copy is.** When token-goat stored the file's contents earlier in the session, from a previous read or from a shell command that printed it, the denial ends with the `token-goat bash-output <id>` that brings that copy back, so the next step is a recall rather than another read.
- **The `commit-msg` hook refuses hard-wrapped commit messages.** GitHub shows a commit body exactly as written, so a paragraph broken at 72 columns reads as lines that stop mid-sentence. The hook now asks for one line per paragraph and per list item, with lists, tables, trailers, indented code and fenced blocks left alone. It also refuses a body made of verification notes ("Mutation check:", "Dogfood:") instead of a description of the change. This affects contributors only and does not ship in the package.
- **The README and docs describe the prompt cache accurately.** They said a whole-file read invalidates the cached conversation prefix and that surgical reads keep a 90%+ cache hit rate. The cache holds the prompt prefix, so appending a tool result invalidates nothing. A large read costs a cache write once, then a cache read on every later turn until the session compacts, and that is what the pages now say. `docs/cli.md` also listed `cache-audit` as checking for prompt-cache busting. It checks that token-goat's hooks are installed and that no environment variable or indexing setting has turned off its caching.
- **`note set` now says what it overwrote.** Setting a key that already had a value replaced it silently, so an earlier finding could vanish without a trace. It now prints the old value on a `Replaced the previous value:` line. When a project already holds 30 notes and the oldest is removed to make room, it names that note on a `Removed the oldest note` line. Setting a key to the value it already has prints nothing extra.
- **The "re-read a section" hint after editing a markdown file appears once per file, not on every edit.** Editing the same file again used to repeat the same hint word for word; one session carried the CHANGELOG.md hint 17 times. It now shows once per file, and again after a compaction, since compaction removes the earlier copy from the conversation.
- **After a compaction, the resume packet says which files the summary left out.** token-goat already checked which of the files in its compaction manifest reappear in the summary Claude Code writes, but only counted them in `stats`. The packet that SessionStart adds after a compaction, and `token-goat resume`, now mark each listed file the summary did not mention with `(not in the compaction summary)`, and list up to 16 more that the packet would not otherwise show. A compaction whose event carries no summary (Codex CLI) clears the list rather than leaving an older one in place.
- **Hook calls and read-only commands find a free hook server when the others are busy.** A call used to try the servers one at a time, giving each 150 ms to answer, so when the first two were busy with slow work the time allowed ran out before the third, idle one was asked, and the hook or command started its own Node process instead. Now the next server is asked 40 ms after the previous one while the call keeps waiting on both, and whichever answers first takes it. Measured on Windows with six `semantic` commands and six hooks fired together, hook calls went from a median of 118 to 175 ms (95th percentile 267 to 499 ms, with up to a third of calls falling back to a fresh Node process at about 500 ms each) to a median of 82 ms (95th percentile 154 ms) with no fallbacks.
- **An edited file is indexed right away instead of at the worker's next poll.** The background worker used to sleep 2 seconds between passes over its queue, so a `symbol`, `read` or `outline` run just after an edit could answer from the old index. The worker now watches its queue folder and starts as soon as an edit hook adds a file; the 2-second poll stays as the fallback wherever the watch cannot run. Measured on one Windows machine, the edited function resolved about 20 ms after the hook queued it, and with the built package the first `symbol` lookup after the hook already found it.
- **`find`, `locate`, `callers`, `call-chain`, `impact`, `dead`, `deps`, `types`, `scope`, `similar`, `context-for` and `test-for` are answered by the resident hook server.** Until now only a shorter list of read commands (`symbol`, `read`, `section`, `outline` and others) skipped the fresh Node start this way; these twelve read the same index and change nothing, but always started a new process. Their output is the same either way. Measured on one Windows machine, `callers` took a median of 100 ms served against 160 ms on its own. `search` still runs on its own, because it prints how long it took, and so does `ask`, which can start an outside model.
- **Installed hook entries written by an older token-goat show in `doctor` as outdated for every harness, and `install` rewrites them.** This covers entries without the exit-code suffix and the Grok form described under Fixed.
- **Project notes show how long ago they were set, and the oldest make room first.** `note set` records when each note was set. The notes block at session start and in the compaction manifest lists the newest first, as `- **key** (set 3h ago): value`, and `note list` shows the same age. At the 30-note limit, a new key replaces the note set longest ago, and when the block reaches its size limit it stops at the first note that does not fit, so the notes left out are always the oldest, and the closing line counts every one of them. Notes saved by an earlier version have no time, show no age, and count as the oldest.
- **If you had `onnxruntime-node` installed, your files are re-embedded once, in the background, after upgrading.** Stored vectors record the runtime that computed them, now `onnxruntime-web@1.30` or `onnxruntime-node@<major.minor>`, and the embedding fingerprint changed with this release. Your existing vectors keep answering searches until each file's new ones replace them. If you did not have it installed, you had no vectors, so nothing is redone: embeddings are computed for the first time. Moving between the two runtimes later re-embeds rather than mixing vectors from both.
- **`scripts/sync-agent-gates.mjs` keeps the read gate in the project's agent files in step with the one `install` writes.** The copies pasted into each agent had fallen behind: they lacked failure shapes, the Commands line and the self-check line. The script renders the gate from the same source `install` uses and writes it between marker comments, so a later run replaces only that block. `--check` writes nothing and exits 1 when a file is out of date. For contributors only; nothing ships in the package.
- **Minified bundles are no longer indexed.** Files named `*.min.js`, `*.min.mjs`, `*.min.cjs`, `*.min.css` or `*-min.js` filled the index with one- and two-letter symbols: a single 166 KB `ort.wasm.min.js` added 262 of them, so `symbol n` returned noise. Bundles already in the index are removed on the next pass, and the source files next to them are untouched. Set `indexing.skip_minified = false` in the global config to index them again; a project's `.token-goat.toml` cannot change this setting.
- **`config set` saves only the settings you changed.** Saving used to write every setting, so one `config set` copied all 123 defaults into `config.toml`, and a later change to a default never reached that install. The file now keeps values that differ from the default, values it already spelled out, and the key just set. A saved `image_shrink.max_image_pixels = 16000000`, the old default, is read as the current default.
- **`doctor` treats a missing config file as normal.** The file is optional and `install` never creates it, yet a fresh install warned that it was missing. Doctor now says there is no config file and the built-in defaults are in use. A file that cannot be parsed still fails.
- **`doctor`'s hook latency check looks only at recent, well-sampled hooks.** One old slow run could keep it warning for the whole retention window. A harness and event pair is now judged only when it has at least 20 calls, one of them in the last 24 hours, and the warning quotes that pair's own call count instead of a total across every hook. A slow pair that misses either test is still mentioned, but the check passes.

### Security

- **Version text from the registry or the update cache must look like a version before it is shown.** It reaches the console, the session-start context the agent reads, and the `stats` insights. A value that is not a semver string is now dropped.
- **Project notes now reach the agent marked as data, not instructions.** A note is whatever someone ran `token-goat note set` with, and it comes back into every new session's context and every compaction manifest. Until now it arrived as plain text beside token-goat's own guidance, so a note that read like an instruction was indistinguishable from one. The notes block is now wrapped in the same fence token-goat already puts around file content (a line saying the content below is data, then `<untrusted-file-content>`), and a note that tries to close that fence early has the tag neutralized. When the compaction manifest's size limit cuts through the notes, the fence is closed before the truncation notice rather than left open over the rest of the manifest. The block still stays within its 4,000-character limit with the fence counted.
- **A large `.token-goat.toml` checked into a repository can no longer stall every hook.** token-goat reads TOML with `smol-toml`, and before 1.9.0 its parser slowed down with the square of the file's length ([GHSA-r4xh-jqrq-34v2](https://github.com/squirrelchat/smol-toml/security/advisories/GHSA-r4xh-jqrq-34v2)). A per-project `.token-goat.toml` arrives with whatever repository you clone and is read on every hook call. Measured on a 1.4 MB file of plain `key = value` lines: 3.2 seconds with 1.8.0, 0.3 seconds with 1.9.0, which token-goat now bundles. `brace-expansion` moved to 5.0.12 as well. token-goat bundles it to match the file patterns you pass to `token-goat pack`, and older versions could hang or crash on a pattern with deeply nested or malformed braces ([GHSA-qhr7-859c-m2p7](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-qhr7-859c-m2p7), [GHSA-6j4f-fj2g-mc7p](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-6j4f-fj2g-mc7p), [GHSA-q2hr-2g5m-vwhr](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-q2hr-2g5m-vwhr)). Those patterns only ever come from your own command line. `fast-uri`, which only the development tools use, moved to its patched version too.
- **Release binaries are signed, attested and checked before publish.** Windows `tg-hook` binaries ship only when the release signs them, with Authenticode, a certificate held in a cloud HSM and an RFC 3161 timestamp, so the signature stays valid after the certificate expires; `Get-AuthenticodeSignature` in PowerShell shows it. A release built without the signing certificate ships no Windows binary at all, so Windows never meets an unsigned program that SmartScreen or an application-control policy might block: it keeps the Node hook command, as macOS does, and `doctor` says so. A signing setup with a typo in its store type or a missing secret stops the release instead of quietly dropping Windows, and `npm pack` or `npm publish` from a working tree refuses to run while `dist/native` holds an unsigned Windows binary, such as the one a local build leaves there, or a Linux one that is not statically linked. The check covers `npm pack` too, so a tarball packed by hand and published later cannot carry what a direct publish would refuse. Both Linux binaries are statically linked (musl) and carry build provenance attestations published to this repository, which `gh attestation verify` checks. Builds are reproducible: the same source gives byte-identical binaries across cargo homes, with no builder path embedded. The publish job rebuilds `dist/native` from the build and signing jobs' artifacts alone and refuses to publish unless exactly the release binaries are present (all four, or just the two Linux ones when the release has no Windows signing set up), each matches the hash its producing job recorded, each Windows file carries an Authenticode signature that names a signer and whose image digest matches the file's own bytes, so a signature lifted from another program is refused, and so is a file with bytes added after its signature, whether past the certificate table, inside one enlarged to hold them, or inside the signature record itself beyond the few zero bytes signers pad it with, or in the parts of the signature the image digest does not cover. The certificates, revocation lists and unsigned attributes, such as the timestamp, must be made of whole DER elements, a second signer beside the first is refused, and the signature's outer structure and its signer must have exactly the fields the Authenticode layout gives them, with the version numbers RFC 5652 allows. The check does not look inside certificates or unsigned attributes, so data wrapped as a whole DER element there still passes. Each Linux file must be a static executable for the right machine, with code to run, that loads no shared library. The certificate chain is checked by the signing job, not here. A `dist/native` entry that cannot be read is reported as a refusal instead of crashing the check, the check still runs when reached through a linked directory such as a global install of a checkout, where before it skipped itself and passed, and the publish step runs the check under `set -euo pipefail` with nothing after it that could swallow its exit status. The signing job runs no repository code and alone holds the signing secrets. How to verify a copy you have: [Verifying what you installed](SECURITY.md#verifying-what-you-installed).
- **`install` refuses hook paths that a harness's shell would rewrite.** A Kimi install path containing `%NAME%` is refused on Windows, and a Grok path containing `$NAME` or `${...}` is refused, since those harnesses' shells would substitute them before the hook runs.
- **`search` masks the values in `.env` files in its text-match previews.** Its text channel read files without the masking and decoding that `read` applies, so a matching line from a `.env` file was printed with its value, and a file saved as UTF-16 never matched at all. It now reads each file the way `read` does.
- **`mcp-output --file` masks the values in a `.env` file, as `bash-output --file` does.** It printed the file as read, leaving only the general secret patterns to catch a value, so a line such as `SMTP_PASS=hunter2` reached the output in full. `bash-output --file` now also reports a missing file as not found rather than as unreadable.
- **`compress-text --file` and `handoff-create --file` mask the values in a `.env` file.** Both saved the file as read, and `retrieve` and `handoff-resolve --full` print back what was saved, so a value whose key the general secret patterns do not recognize came back in full. The file is now masked as it is read in, as `bash-output --file` and `mcp-output --file` do.
- **With `indexing.cross_project_symbols` off, the MCP `read`, `brief`, `skeleton`, `outline` and `exports` tools no longer reach another project because a call names its root.** A `projectRoot` naming another project was taken as the project to confine the lookup to, so a caller in one project could read the indexed code of any other, including files since deleted from disk. These tools now follow the rule the `symbol` and `refs` tools already did: a named root counts only when it sits inside the current project or `mcp.allowed_roots` lists it, and any other is refused.
- **With `indexing.cross_project_symbols` off, `search --project` and the MCP `semantic`, `map` and `changed` tools no longer answer from another project's index.** Each searched whatever root it was given, so from inside one project, `search --project` and the MCP `semantic` tool returned another project's code, including files since deleted from disk, `map` listed its headline symbols, and `changed` in symbol mode named the symbols in its changed files. A root outside the current project is now refused unless `mcp.allowed_roots` lists it, the rule `symbol` already follows. The file list `changed` prints without symbol mode comes from git rather than the index and still answers.
- **An Azure storage `AccountKey` on its own line in an MCP call's arguments is masked in the label saved for that call.** The label is built from the arguments as JSON text, which writes a line break as `\n`, and the pattern for this key could not begin a match right after one, so the key stayed in the label that `bash-history` lists and `recall` searches.
- **`xlsx-query --json` masks secrets in a sheet's header row, and a header that reads like an instruction to the model comes wrapped in the notice that says it is untrusted.** The header cells name the fields of every row, and they were printed as the sheet had them, while the cells under them were already masked and checked. The output still parses. `mcp-output --json-query --json` now treats the field names it prints the same way.
- **Text in a `--json` report that imitates token-goat's untrusted-content notice is escaped like the rest of the text the report quotes.** A string value that opened with a `[token-goat: ...]` line over one of the tags token-goat wraps untrusted text in passed through unescaped, so a spreadsheet cell or a saved MCP result could speak in token-goat's voice, in that first line and in any line under it. Such a value now passes unescaped only when its first line is a notice token-goat writes, and the text under it is escaped the way token-goat escapes text it wraps itself.
- **On Windows, the Bash hook no longer contacts a file server that a command names before the command is approved.** Before approval, the hook measures some of the files a command reads to decide what to suggest in its place: the file behind a `sed -n` or `awk` line range, behind a `head -n`, `Get-Content -TotalCount` or `Select-Object -First` read, and behind an `rg` or `grep` search the index can answer. Measuring a path such as `//server/share/x.ts` opened a connection to that server and offered it an NTLM authentication, and a server that did not answer held the command for about 21 seconds. These paths now go through the check the hook's other file lookups use, which leaves a network path unmeasured, and such a command starts in under 200 milliseconds.
- **The optional embedding runtime installs with no known advisory from `onnxruntime-node` 1.30.0 onward.** Earlier releases pull in `adm-zip` 0.5, which carries a ZIP-bomb advisory that only `onnxruntime-node`'s own install script can reach. 1.30.0 asks for 0.6, and a fresh install resolves 0.6.1, which also clears the symlink advisory in 0.6.0. [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md#dependency-advisories) now says so and keeps the `overrides` fix for anyone held on an older release. This repository's own `npm audit` is clean again: `adm-zip` and `hono` moved to their patched versions once each had cleared the seven-day cooldown.
- **Every embedding download refuses to run in offline mode at the point where the request is made.** The model and the WebAssembly runtime each check `network.offline` before downloading, and the shared download function now checks it too, so a future caller that forgets its own check still cannot reach the network in offline mode.
- **`pdf-locate` and `pptx-text --grep` no longer leak part of a credential that sits at the edge of a snippet.** Both commands cut a short window of text around each match and only then checked it for secrets. When a key straddled the end of that window, the piece left inside was too short for the redaction patterns to recognise, so its first ten or so characters reached the output. The page or slide text is now redacted before it is searched and cut.
- **Cached output recalled with `--grep` is redacted before long lines are clipped.** Matching lines were clipped at 1,000 characters before the secret redactor ran, so a secret cut at the clip point no longer looked like one and printed in part. Redaction now runs first, ahead of every narrowing option, and that covers the Office document commands that share the same path.
- **Passwords in URLs are redacted when they contain `:` or `@`, and so are Basic auth headers with extra spaces.** A password containing either character was left in the clear or only partly redacted, and a `Basic` header with more than one space before the token was not redacted at all. Both are caught now, and an email address or quoted value right after the URL is still left alone.
- **A custom redaction pattern that can match empty text is rejected.** Only patterns that matched the empty string were refused, so a pattern such as a word boundary was accepted and then put a redaction marker at every word boundary in the output. Such patterns are now reported with the other pattern problems, and empty matches are skipped.
- **A password in a URL with an empty username, or with an email address as the username, is redacted.** A connection string such as `redis://:password@host:6379/0`, which redis and Celery use when there is no username, kept its password in plain text, and so did `smtp://bob@example.com:password@host`. Both forms are redacted now, while `ssh://git@github.com:2222/repo` and an email address after a URL with a port are still left alone.
- **`xlsx-columns` redacts sample values before cutting them to the column width.** Each column's sample value was cut to 27 characters and then printed without a redaction pass, so the first 27 characters of an API key stored in a spreadsheet cell reached the output. The sample is now redacted first and cut afterwards.
- **A custom redaction pattern that matches only empty text no longer blocks file summaries.** A pattern such as `(?<=token:)` passes the empty-match check when it is compiled, yet on real text it can only match empty, so it never redacts anything. The check that decides whether a file holds a real secret still counted those empty matches, which stopped a large file from being summarized whenever the text contained `token:`. Only matches with content count now.

### Fixed

- A DuckDB macro or other SQL function with no body block, such as `CREATE FUNCTION add_two(a) AS a + 2;`, ran on in `read` and `outline` past its own `;` and swallowed the statements below it. Such a function now ends at its first `;`. Functions with a `BEGIN`, `DECLARE`, dollar-quoted or PL/SQL `IS`/`AS` body still run to the end of that body. An `IS NULL` test or a `CAST(x AS type)` inside the body, or a column quoted as `"begin"`, no longer counts as the start of a body block.
- `--lines` used with `--section` on `bash-output`, `web-output`, `mcp-output` and `retrieve` counted from the section's first line, while `-n` labels lines counted from the top of the stored text. A range copied from `-n` output therefore pointed at the wrong lines. `--lines` now uses the same numbers `-n` prints, and a range outside the section is refused with the lines the section covers.
- `token-goat install` for Zed wrote its launcher script and then updated `settings.json`. If that write failed (the file read-only, or held open by Zed), the new launcher was left in place with nothing pointing at it. The launcher is now put back as it was before the error is reported, byte for byte and with its old file permissions, and a launcher that existed but could not be read is never deleted.
- `aws` and `az` output, and `npm audit --json`, were left unshrunk when one string in the JSON was very long, such as a log message, a policy document or an advisory. Clipping that one line made the document unreadable as JSON, so the array and vulnerability-map shortening never ran. These commands now read the whole document before clipping, including `--filter aws` and coloured `az` output, and `npm audit --json` reads the document from standard output only, so an `npm warn` line on standard error no longer breaks it.
- A config file that starts with a UTF-8 byte-order mark (Notepad, PowerShell 5 and Visual Studio all write one) lost the mark when `token-goat install` added its entry to it. The mark is now kept for Codex, Copilot, Cursor, Kimi, Visual Studio, VS Code and Zed config files.
- `semantic` named a hit after the first symbol in it even when the hit ran on into the next symbols, which happens on small files and when two nearby hits are merged. A hit that covers more than one symbol is now labelled with the symbol that contains all of it, or with no symbol when none does. Two symbols sharing a line, top-level code after the last symbol, and a file that can no longer be read are all handled the same way.
- `cache-audit` reported `TOKEN_GOAT_BASH_COMPRESS=off` or `=no` as enabled, although token-goat itself reads those values as off. It now reads the variable the same way token-goat does, and its label says the setting turns off compression only, since output is still cached for `bash-output` and `recall`.
- `retrieve <id>` and `retrieve <id> --full` promised the original bytes back, but removed colour codes and turned Windows line endings into Unix ones. Both now print the stored text exactly as it was saved, without adding a final newline the original did not have.
- `config-get` on a TOML file split the key on every dot, so a quoted key such as `site."google.com"` could not be read, and when a `[site.google]` table also existed the unquoted spelling found the table instead. Quoted segments are now read as one key, with TOML's escape rules applied, and are never joined with the segments around them. A key that carries text of its own, such as `a = 0 #`, is reported missing instead of being read as `a`.
- On a slow machine, the start-of-session check for files changed outside the session could spend its whole time limit listing the project and checking nothing, then start from the same place next time, so changed files were never reindexed. The time limit now starts once the list is ready.
- `aws` and `az` JSON output was only shortened when the long list sat at the top or one level down. `aws ec2 describe-instances` puts every instance of one launch inside a single reservation, so an Auto Scaling group's hundreds of instances came through in full. Long lists nested deeper are now shortened the same way.
- `compress --filter` with a name no filter has, such as `--filter az` for the Azure CLI, quietly fell back to guessing a filter, so the output came back unshrunk with no hint why. It now says on standard error that there is no filter by that name, what ran instead, and the names it may have meant, such as `azure-cli`.
- `note set` and `note list` printed a note's value as stored, so a value copied from a web page or tool output could send escape sequences to the terminal or break the list across lines. They now show control characters escaped on one line. `note get` still returns the value exactly as saved, for scripts.
- A Markdown heading indented one to three spaces (for example `  ### Title`) was missing from `outline` and `section`, and it did not end the section above it. It is now indexed like any other heading.
- A `# comment` inside TOML front matter fenced with `+++` (Hugo and Zola sites) showed up as a heading in `outline`. The `+++` block is now skipped, as `---` front matter already was.
- A heading written over several lines, such as two text lines above `=====`, was listed under its last line only and started one line late. It is now named after the whole paragraph and starts on its first line.
- `section --grep` showed a matching line under the wrong sub-heading when that sub-heading was indented one to three spaces, and could show a `# comment` from a code block as if it were a heading. Indented headings now count and lines inside code blocks never do.
- In a Dockerfile, `outline` and `read` showed a `RUN` command split across several lines as a single line, so `read Dockerfile:5` returned a gap instead of the command. A `RUN <<EOT ... EOT` block also made lines inside it, such as `FROM fake`, appear as real instructions. Continued commands and heredoc blocks now cover all their lines, and nothing inside a heredoc is listed as an instruction.
- A multi-line `CREATE TABLE`, `CREATE VIEW` or similar SQL statement was reported as running past its closing semicolon, so reading it also printed the INSERT, UPDATE and GRANT lines, comments and blank lines that followed. Each such statement now ends at its own semicolon, and function and procedure bodies no longer include trailing blank or comment lines.
- A Makefile line like `build: CFLAGS += -g` was listed as a second `build` target, so `read Makefile::build` failed as ambiguous. Target-specific variable assignments (`=`, `:=`, `::=`, `?=`, `+=` and `!=`, with or without `override` or `export`) are no longer listed as targets.
- `web-output`, `mcp-output` and `bash-output --section` on a heading that appears more than once printed only the first match and gave no sign there were others. They now refuse and list each match's line with the `--section "Heading#N"` form that picks it, as `section` does.
- `-n` together with `--section` on `web-output`, `mcp-output` or `bash-output` numbered the section's lines from 1, so a follow-up `--lines` built from those numbers returned the wrong text. The numbers now match the whole stored output, the same as `--lines` and `-n` without `--section`.
- `eslint -f json` output compressed to the first 2,000 characters of the first file, with no other files and no totals. Compressed ESLint JSON now shows every file with problems, one `line:col severity message rule` row per problem, and the error and warning totals.
- Compressed `gh api` output for a large response came back as a cut-off fragment of the JSON, with none of the clutter removed, and file contents were shown as raw base64. Large `gh api` responses now have their boilerplate URL fields stripped and file contents replaced with a short size note. The same goes for one-line arrays from the `json` tool.
- Blocking a second, different web page in the same session no longer reports "Repeat refusal of this exact call". Only a call with the same URL and prompt is shortened that way; a different call gets the full reason.
- A rejected `install --zed` (a settings.json that will not parse, or an existing `token-goat` entry that token-goat did not write) no longer leaves `token-goat-mcp.cmd` behind. `uninstall --zed` now removes that shim even when settings.json cannot be parsed, while still reporting the parse error.
- `image-meta` no longer calls a 16-bit or interlaced PNG "already small/optimal". It now says `Shrink: not attempted` with the reason, and `--json` carries the reason in a new `shrinkRefusal` field.
- Compressed ESLint output put the blank line that separates two files between one file's errors and its warnings, so the next file's path ran straight on from the last warning. The separator now comes after each file's last row.
- **`token-goat semantic` and the combined search could show a whole file as one result, previewing its first lines and naming the wrong function.** This happened even when one function further down was the match. Each result now shows the best-matching chunk's own lines, preview and enclosing function, and neighbouring functions are no longer merged into one span.
- **Searching saved command output missed text that changed colour mid-phrase.** This affected `bash-output --grep`, `bash-output --section` and `recall`, for example on `error TS2322` in `tsc --pretty` output. These searches now match the plain text you see, and recall snippets no longer show stray escape codes. `bash-output --full` now prints plain text, without colour.
- **Compressed colored output could show a lone `(×2)` line where blank lines had been collapsed.** Color-heavy test and type-check output now gets its normal tool-specific compression, and the footer no longer credits a filter that did not run.
- **`read` and `brief` could not reach a key in a dotted TOML table, such as `pyproject.toml::tool.ruff.line-length` or Cargo's `dependencies.tokio.version`.** The ambiguity message also suggested specs that then failed. Dotted table names now resolve, and every retry the message suggests works.
- **A multi-line project note broke the notes block shown at session start.** Each note now stays on one line, with line breaks shown as `\n`. `note list --json` still returns the exact text.
- **`note unset` on a key that does not exist now prints `Key not found` and exits 1, as `note get` does.**
- **Installing and then uninstalling token-goat rewrote your Claude `settings.json`.** Windows line endings became Unix ones and tab indentation became two spaces, so every line showed as changed in a diff. Install and uninstall now keep the file's line endings, indentation and trailing newline (or lack of one), and uninstall returns the file byte for byte as it was. The same applies to the Antigravity and JetBrains settings files.
- **`session-audit` reported a transcript as unreadable when one line was valid JSON but not an object, such as `null`, or held a message with an unexpected shape.** The lines before it were still counted. Those lines are now skipped like any other malformed line, and a file that does fail no longer leaves partial counts in the report.
- **The Copilot waste report failed on an events log that contained a `null` line.** It now skips that line.
- **`token-goat mcp-audit` missed MCP servers added at user scope, such as with `claude mcp add --scope user`, and listed only one file's servers when several scopes declared some.** It now reads local, project and user scope together, as Claude Code does, and the "Config found" line names every file it read. A name declared in more than one scope is listed once. A recorded call to an undeclared server whose name matches a built-in JavaScript property, such as `toString`, is now listed as unconfigured instead of being skipped.
- **`token-goat read "README.md::Usage"` and `brief` printed only the heading line of a markdown section.** The header still claimed the whole section, for example "7 lines" over a single line. Both commands and their `--json` output now print the full section, and `brief --json` includes the body for HTML headings too.
- **Swift files with an interpolation such as `"\(name.isEmpty ? "}" : name)"` lost every symbol after that line.** Members after it were also attached to the wrong type. The brace inside the interpolated string no longer counts as code. Upgrading reindexes.
- **Kotlin, Scala, Dart, C# and Swift classes and methods came out cut short, or ran into the next symbol, when an interpolation held a nested string containing `{` or `}`.** For example `"${x.replace("}", "")}"` in Kotlin or `$"{d["}"]}"` in C#. Upgrading reindexes.
- **Dart single-quoted strings with a `${...}` hole holding a brace, and Scala plain strings containing `${`, no longer confuse the symbol index.** Only an `s"..."`-style interpolator opens a hole in Scala, as in the compiler.
- **Compressed pytest output on Windows kept every repeated warning.** A warning whose location starts with a drive letter, such as `C:\project\test_x.py:6:`, now collapses to one line the way it already did on Linux and macOS.
- **Compressed pytest output dropped lines a failing test printed when they held only `.`, `F`, `E` or `s` characters.** Those lines looked like the progress bar. They now stay under the failure's captured output.
- **`token-goat mcp-audit` reported "Config found: no" when `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `COPILOT_HOME` was set.** It now reads `.claude.json` from `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `mcp-config.json` from `COPILOT_HOME`, and the line that names what it checked shows those real paths.
- **Installing into a CLAUDE.md or copilot-instructions.md with Windows line endings left the file with mixed line endings, and uninstalling did not give the original back.** The block now uses the file's own line endings, so an install followed by an uninstall restores the file byte for byte.
- **`install -p` still wrote the CLAUDE.md block and skill into your home directory.** With `-p`/`--project` they now go to `<project>/CLAUDE.md` and `<project>/.claude/skills/token-goat/`, and `uninstall -p` takes them back out of the project without touching the user-wide copies.
- **`uninstall -p` left an empty `.claude` folder behind in the project.** A settings file that now holds nothing, the empty `skills` folder and the `.claude` folder above them are removed when this install created them. A `.claude` folder you already had, and anything you keep in it, stays.
- **Uninstalling the VS Code, Cursor, Visual Studio, Copilot or Zed MCP server deleted an empty `servers` block you had written yourself.** Uninstall now removes that block only when install added it.
- **`doctor` reported a damaged global.db as healthy.** It now runs SQLite's own integrity check, so a database with malformed pages fails the Database check, and `doctor --repair` moves a damaged or unreadable file aside (never deleting it) and starts a fresh one for the index to refill.
- **The MCP server crashed on a request line that was valid JSON but not an object,** such as `null` or a batch array. It now answers with the standard "Invalid Request" error and keeps running.
- **`doctor --repair` could leave an empty CLAUDE.md in your project.** When it created a project CLAUDE.md to hold the routing gate, it did not note that it had made the file, so `uninstall -p` took the block out and left the empty file behind. It now removes that file. A CLAUDE.md you wrote yourself keeps everything you put in it.
- **`doctor` failed an empty global.db and sent you to `--repair`.** SQLite treats a 0-byte file as a new database, and token-goat fills it in on next use, so there is nothing to recover. `doctor` now warns that the file is empty and tells you to run `token-goat index`, and `--repair` leaves it in place.
- **Callers that import a symbol by package name were missing from `refs --callers`, `callers`, `brief` and `impact`.** In a monorepo, packages import each other as `@scope/pkg`, which the reference check could not resolve, so it treated those calls as reaching some other function and dropped them. They are now listed like callers that use a relative import.
- **The broad-glob hint did not fire for a `**/*` Glob on the project root.** Claude Code sends the root as an absolute path, and only `.` or an empty path was recognised. The hint that suggests `token-goat map --compact` now appears for both.
- **`token-goat symbol` told you to pass `-p` when every match was already in this project.** The note now appears only when some matches live in other projects, where `-p` actually narrows the list.
- **`--top 0` and `--top -2` gave contradictory messages.** On `refs`, `impact`, `dead`, `similar`, `context-for`, `coverage-gaps`, `arch` and `ask`, a negative value said "must be a non-negative number" while `0` said "must be a positive number". Both now say `--top must be a positive number`.
- **`--grep` that matched nothing printed a blank line and exited 0.** That looked the same as an empty result. `bash-output`, `web-output`, `mcp-output`, `retrieve`, `recall` and the document commands now report `--grep matched no lines of N` and exit 1, as `--section` already does for a heading it cannot find.
- **A 32-bit BMP saved by a Windows capture tool reached the model fully transparent.** Windows leaves the fourth byte of each pixel at 0, and token-goat read it as alpha, so the shrunk image was blank. That byte now counts as alpha only when the file declares an alpha mask or some pixel actually uses it.
- **A truncated BMP was shown to the model as a mostly black image.** A BMP whose pixel data stops short is now treated like every other truncated image: the read passes through unchanged.
- **Haskell functions written with guards were missing or cut short.** A function whose head line had no `=` because its body was a set of guards on the following lines was left out of the index, or only its type signature was returned. A signature continued onto lines that start with `::` or `->` was cut short in the same way. Both now index and read as whole definitions.
- **Haskell and OCaml ranges ran one line past the end of the file, and a shared Haskell signature listed a name twice.** The last definition in a file now ends on its last line, and `baz, qux :: Int` lists each name once.
- **Clojure definitions with reader metadata were indexed under the metadata.** `(defn ^:private helper ...)`, `^String`, `^{:doc ...}` and `#^` now index under the real name, so `read` and `symbol` find them.
- **An OCaml nested module lost its closing `end`.** `module Inner = struct ... end` now includes the `end` line in its range.
- **`uninstall -p` and `uninstall --all --project` also removed your personal setup.** They now touch only the project, and print a note naming the setups that live in your user profile and were left alone.
- **`doctor` and `doctor --repair` stopped partway when the stats database was damaged.** They now report the damaged database and still run every other check.
- **Installing into a settings file that was a symlink replaced the link with a plain file.** Install now writes through the link to the real file, which matters for settings kept in a dotfiles repo. A link owned by another user is not followed.
- **`config-get` could not read most TOML files.** It now handles arrays and strings that span several lines, dotted keys such as `server.port = 8080`, and inline tables such as `owner = { name = "n" }`.
- **`changed --symbol` listed files that were only renamed or had only their file mode changed.** Those files have no changed lines, so they are now left out.
- **Reading a large shell script got no hint to read one function instead.** The Read hint now offers `token-goat read "script.sh::function"` for shell scripts, as it already did for other languages.
- **The Grep hint could not name a symbol with a hyphen in it or a markdown heading.** A search for either now points at the matching `read` or `section` command.
- **`refs --callers` left out calls made from arrow functions stored on a class property or from functions stored in an object.** Those calls are now credited to the property or key name, the same way a method's calls are. Upgrading reindexes each project once to pick this up.
- **An Elixir `def name(args),` with `do:` on the next line was indexed as one line.** The function now gets its full span, so `read` returns its body.
- **Replacing an indexed file with a folder of the same name left the old file's symbols in the index.** Reconcile and the background worker now drop the stale file, so `symbol` stops finding code that is gone.
- **A failed `cargo build` kept the warnings and cut the error.** Compressed output now keeps every `error[...]` block whole, with its source location and notes, and folds the warnings into one line that says how many there were.
- **`image-meta` called a valid AVIF file unreadable and exited 1.** It now reports the width, height and format of AVIF, HEIC and HEIF images, and says that token-goat does not re-encode them.
- **`install --copilot --project` and `install --pi --project` wrote the user-scope config.** Both only listened to `--local`. Now `-p`/`--project` picks the project-local target too (`<project>/.github/hooks/token-goat.json`, `<project>/.pi/extensions/token-goat.ts`), on install and on uninstall, and the help text says so.
- **A freshly `git init`-ed project was reported as "not a git repository".** When a project has no tracked files yet but is its own git top level, install now says exactly that, and names `git add` followed by `token-goat index`, or `token-goat index --walk`, as the way to get it indexed.
- **`config-get` finds dotted keys in `.properties` files and `export`ed keys in `.env` files.** A key such as `spring.datasource.url` was split at its first dot into a section and a key, which `.properties` files do not have, so the lookup came back empty. Lines written as `key: value` or `key value` were also missed, and so was `export KEY=value` in a `.env` file. The whole key is now tried first, and all three separators and the `export` prefix are understood.
- **The Grep hint names the symbol you searched for.** A search for `function beta` in an indexed file pointed at whichever symbol the file listed first. The hint now picks the symbol the pattern names.
- **Deny hints only name commands that work on the file in question.** A `.properties` file was offered `outline`, a `.rst` file `outline`, a `.txt` file `section`, and a stylesheet `section`, none of which return anything for those files. They now get `config-get` or a grep, `section`, a grep, and `outline` respectively.
- **Compressed `docker build` output keeps the whole failing step.** The filter kept the `ERROR:` line and dropped the step's earlier lines, so the compiler error, missing module or npm 404 that explained the failure was gone. Every line of a step that ends in an error now survives, and successful steps are still collapsed.
- **`changed --symbol` lists only the edited symbols of a file that was renamed and edited.** Git reported the new path as a brand-new file, so every symbol in it showed up as changed. The diff now detects renames and compares the file against its old path.
- **`semantic --warm` with no query reports whether embeddings are ready.** It used to demand a query and exit with a usage error, so the warm-up the hint recommends could not be run on its own.
- **`install`, `uninstall` and `doctor` failed with "malformed MCP JSON" for Cursor, VS Code, Visual Studio, Zed and Copilot CLI when the config file started with a UTF-8 byte-order mark.** Notepad, PowerShell 5 and Visual Studio can save files that way, and `doctor` then reported token-goat as not installed. These files, and the Claude Code, Grok, Antigravity, Copilot CLI and JetBrains configs, are now read with the mark ignored. When token-goat rewrites one of them, the mark is dropped and the rest of the file is kept.
- **`refs` found no use of a React component that was only rendered, and `dead` listed it as unused.** JSX tags such as `<Button />`, `<Card>` and `<UI.Panel>` are now recorded as references to the component. Lowercase tags like `<div>` are left out.
- **A Java `new Pair<>(a, b)` was recorded as a reference to `Pair<>`, so `refs Pair` missed it.** The type arguments are now dropped and the reference is stored under `Pair`.
- **`read "file.lua::Queue.push"` could not find `function Queue:push` or `function Account.new`.** These functions are now indexed with their table as the parent, so the qualified name resolves.
- **Dart operator overloads such as `Point operator +(Point other)` were missing from the index.** They are now indexed under the operator symbol with the class as parent, and `read "shape.dart::operator +"` finds them.
- **Copilot CLI and VS Code project hooks were rewritten, with a new backup each time, when `install` or `doctor --repair` ran from a folder spelled with a lower-case drive letter.** `c:\proj` and `C:\proj` are the same folder on Windows. The drive letter is now upper-cased before the hook commands are written, so the file stays as it was.
- **`doctor` said Claude Code's settings.json had probably been overwritten or deleted when only one hook event was missing.** That message is now kept for settings with no token-goat hook at all, and a partly wired file gets the row that names the missing events.
- **`uninstall` left behind the empty config files and folders that install had created for Gemini CLI, Qwen Code, OpenClaw, Kimi, Codex, Grok, Cursor, opencode, pi and Zed.** Install now records what it creates, and uninstall removes those files and folders once nothing else is in them. A file or folder that was there before install stays.
- **Compressed `go test -v` output attached a failing test's log lines to the test before it and kept every passing test's `t.Log` lines.** Go prints a test's log lines between its `=== RUN` and its verdict, which the filter did not follow. Log lines now stay with their own test, and those from passing tests are dropped.
- **`session-outline` and `session-slice` skipped any transcript turn containing U+2028 or U+2029 and numbered every later turn one too low.** The transcript reader treated those characters as line breaks. They now stay inside the turn, and turn numbers match the transcript.
- **`yaml-query` and `yaml-outline` read a YAML file ending in `---` or in a comment-only document as a list of two documents.** Every path then needed a `0.` in front. Empty documents are now ignored, so a file with one real document reads as that document.
- **`skeleton` and `search` showed the decorator or annotation line instead of the declaration.** A decorated Python, TypeScript or Java symbol came back as `@dataclass`, `@Override` or `@Get(':id')`. Both now skip leading decorators and show the signature line.
- **`search` merged separate definitions that share a name in one file into one result with a made-up line range.** Two `render` methods in different classes came back as a single hit spanning both, and the second disappeared. Each definition is now its own result.
- **The pre-read notice for a large JSON or YAML file printed "command omitted: the path contains shell metacharacters" in place of its query command, even for a plain path.** The check was tripping on the `'<path>'` placeholder rather than the file path. The notice now names a `json-query` or `yaml-query` command you can paste.
- **A markdown file with any setext heading (a title underlined with `===` or `---`) made the pre-read section hint name the whole-document title.** Setext headings now count at their real level, so the hint names the section you were about to read.
- **The pre-read hint for a large markdown file said "40 headings" for a file with 301.** It reported the number it listed rather than the number in the file. It now gives the real count and says how many it left out.
- **Ruby methods called without parentheses or a receiver were never recorded as references.** A bare `helper` or `save!` inside another method counts as a call in Ruby, yet `refs --callers`, `impact` and `dead` saw nothing calling it. These calls are now indexed like `helper()`.
- **Scala definitions written as `def f(...) =` with the body on the next line, and every Scala 3 type declared with a colon and an indented body, were stored as one line.** `read` returned the signature alone. The span now runs to the end of the body.
- **Elixir functions whose `do` sat on a later line were stored as their first line.** `mix format` wraps long argument lists and `when` guards that way, so `read` returned only `def name(`. The span now starts at `def` and runs to the matching `end`.
- **`token-goat answer` refused any subject written as `file::Class.method`.** It now resolves the subject the way `read` does, and when the name matches more than one definition it lists each one with the spelling that picks it.
- **`token-goat impact` skipped a caller that shared the symbol's name, and later hops followed the callers of unrelated methods with the same name.** Each step now looks only at references to the definition it is expanding.
- **`callers`, `brief`, `impact` and `call-chain` dropped a TypeScript call when the calling file defined its own method of the same name.** They now check what the call is bound to first, as `refs` already did, and keep the call when it reaches the symbol you asked about.
- **`token-goat dead` listed a method as unused when every file that called it also defined a method of the same name.** It now checks what each call is bound to before reporting a method, and answers the whole scan from one TypeScript program, so the check adds seconds rather than minutes on a large project.
- **`callers`, `impact`, `call-chain` and `refs` on an overloaded `file::Class.method` silently picked one overload, and `refs` with several symbols printed "no references found" for it.** They now say the name is ambiguous and list each overload with its `@line` spelling.
- **`token-goat dead` and `coverage-gaps` listed C# and C++ operators, conversions, destructors and indexers as unused or untested.** These run from syntax such as `a + b`, a cast or the end of a scope, so no call site ever names them and a count of name references cannot tell whether they are used. Both commands now leave them out, and still report an unused method in the same class.
- **C#, Kotlin, PHP and PowerShell methods written on one line were stored cut at the first brace.** `public int Get() { return x; }` came back from `read` as `public int Get()`. The whole line is now the method's body.
- **Kotlin functions written as `fun f() = ...` across several lines were stored as their first line only.** The body now runs to the end of the expression, following trailing operators, commas, chained calls and `if`/`else` branches.
- **C# operator overloads, conversions, finalizers and indexers were missing from the index or filed under the wrong name.** They are now indexed as `operator+`, `operator int`, `~Calc` and `this[]`, the same names C++ uses, so `read "Calc.cs::Calc.operator+"` finds them.
- **A Rust `impl<T> Wrapper<T>` block was named `Wrapper<T>`, so `read "lib.rs::Wrapper.new"` found nothing.** Impl blocks are now named after the bare type: `impl<T> Wrapper<T>`, `impl foo::Bar` and `impl Trait for &Foo` resolve as `Wrapper`, `Bar` and `Foo`.
- **Compressed `git diff` output dropped hunks that changed indentation or spaces inside a string, calling them whitespace-only.** A Python block moved one level in, or `"a b"` turned into `"a  b"`, changes what the code does, yet the hunk was folded away. Only trailing spaces, line endings and spacing around operators outside quotes now count as whitespace-only.
- **`pdf-extract` and `docx-text` rejected the `--lines` and `--full` flags that their own output told you to use.** The note at the end of a long extract named both flags, and running either one failed with an unknown-option error. Both commands accept them now, and a test checks every command that prints that note against the flags it names.
- **`xml-query //a//b` returned the same element more than once when `b` sat under nested `a` elements, and `//a/@id` read only the top match.** Later `//` steps now search descendants, results come back once each in file order, and an attribute step after `//` reads every matching element.
- **`token-goat install --gemini` and `--qwen` refused a settings.json that held comments, `doctor` called it not installed, and `install --openclaw` dropped the comments from `openclaw.json`.** Gemini CLI and Qwen Code accept comments in settings.json, and OpenClaw reads JSON5, so a hand-commented file is normal. Install, uninstall and `doctor` now read these files, and every change keeps the comments where they were. A trailing comma in settings.json is still refused, as Gemini CLI and Qwen Code refuse it.
- **`token-goat uninstall --vscode --project` left behind the `.github/copilot-instructions.md` that the install created.** Install now records the file, and the `.github` folder when it made that too, and uninstall removes them once token-goat's block is gone and nothing else is in them. A file or folder that was there before install keeps its content.
- **`token-goat stats --payloads` measures the skill description that is actually installed.** The skill row rebuilt the Kimi Code skill's longer description line instead of reading the Claude Code skill, so its size was wrong. It now reads the `description:` line from the installed SKILL.md. A comment in the installer that said the Claude Code skill used that shared line is corrected, and a test checks that both descriptions open with the same routing pitch.
- **`xml-query --xpath` counted positions from 0, so `/root/item[1]` returned the second item and `/root/item[3]` returned nothing.** Positions now start at 1 as in XPath, `[0]` matches nothing, and `//b[1]` picks the first `b` under each parent. The dotted path form still counts from 0.
- **`xml-query` printed all of an element's text before its child elements, so `<p>One<b>bold</b>Two</p>` came out as "One", "Two", then `<b>bold</b>`.** Text and child elements now print in the order they appear in the file.
- **`xml-query` exited 0 when nothing matched, unlike `json-query` and `yaml-query`.** It now prints the "No elements matched" or "No attributes matched" message on stderr and exits 1.
- **The "showing first N of M matching lines" note from `bash-output`, `web-output` and `mcp-output --grep --max-matches` came out escaped as `&#91;token-goat: ...` inside the untrusted-content block.** It now appears unescaped on stderr after the block.
- **In C and C++ files, a forward declaration such as `struct node;` or a use of a struct, class, union or enum name showed up as if it were a definition, so looking up the type could land on the wrong line.** Only the real definition, the one with a body, is listed now.
- **In JavaScript files, functions attached with `exports.name = function`, `module.exports.name = function`, `Thing.prototype.name = function`, or `module.exports = function name(){}` were missing from outlines and symbol lookups.** They are listed now, and prototype methods are filed under their owner.
- **In C++ files, destructors and operator overloads written inside a class, such as `~Widget()` or `operator==`, were missing from outlines.** They are listed now, under the class that owns them, with the same names whether they are written inside or outside the class. Functions that return a reference, such as `Widget& operator=`, are listed as well.
- **In YAML and TOML files, reading a top-level key or a `[table]` printed only its header line, and an edit inside the block was not attributed to it.** Keys now cover their whole block, and tables run through their last key. Quoted keys and keys that start with a digit, such as `"my key":` or `2024:`, were missing and are listed now. Upgrading reindexes existing projects.
- **Running install on a Claude Code settings.json where a hook event such as `PreToolUse` was a plain string or a single object instead of a list either corrupted it or failed with "not iterable", and uninstall and the doctor check failed the same way.** Your value is now left exactly as you wrote it, install tells you which event it skipped, and uninstall and doctor run normally.
- **Uninstalling the Codex integration deleted every hook trust entry for your Codex config, including trust you had granted to your own hooks, so Codex asked you to approve them again.** Only the entries belonging to token-goat's hooks are removed now, and your own entries are renamed when removing token-goat's hooks moves them up the list.
- **Installing and then uninstalling the Codex integration stripped every comment and the original layout from `~/.codex/config.toml`, and uninstall deleted the backup that held them.** Install now adds its hooks in a marked block at the end of the file and uninstall removes just that block, so the file comes back exactly as it was and anything you edited in between stays. If the file ever has to be rewritten without its comments, the backup is kept.
- **Reading `file::Square.area` when the file had no `Square` class printed the body of a different class's `area` method as if it were the one you asked for.** A wrong class name now reports that the symbol was not found and suggests the real spelling, such as `Circle.area`. The same applies to `refs`, `callers`, `call-chain`, `impact` and `brief`.
- **A method named through several class levels, such as `Outer.Inner.run`, ignored the levels in the middle, so it could resolve to the wrong class or report a clash between two nested classes with the same name.** The whole chain of enclosing names now has to match.
- **Search results could show a class as spanning lines it does not cover: a class on lines 1 to 3 appeared as lines 1 to 9 because a nearby match was folded into it.** Each result now keeps its own lines, and a nearby match is attributed to the smallest symbol that contains it.
- **`callers`, `impact`, `call-chain` and `brief` looked for callers in the project you were standing in, not the project the named file belongs to, so a file path pointing into a sibling project reported "not found" or zero callers.** They now search the project that owns the file.
- **CSV and Excel columns with a blank header get a name, and the office readers keep merged cells and Word headings.** A blank header cell came through as an empty column name that `--columns` could not select; it is now named `colN` after its position, with a `_` added if that name is taken. `docx-text --section` found only sections that already began with `#`, so a Word document's Title and Heading paragraphs never matched; it now marks them as headings and finds them. `docx-tables` read a cell merged across columns as one cell, which moved every later cell in the row one column left; it now fills the spanned columns with empty cells. `xlsx-query` accepts `--no-header`, the flag its own duplicate-header error tells you to use. The summary built before Claude Code compacts a conversation no longer lists a file you only edited as a read you can drop.
- **Bash functions whose body is a subshell, `if`, loop, `case` or `[[ ]]` stop at the end of that body, and multi-line C# `=>` members span to their `;`.** The bash reader only knew brace-bodied functions, so `f() ( ... )` or `f() if ...; fi` ran on to the next brace and swallowed the function after it, and `read` returned both. It now finds the end of each kind of body, and a `case` pattern's `)` inside a subshell does not end the function early. A C# method or property written as `=> expression` over several lines was one line long, so `read` cut it off; it now ends at the `;` that closes the expression, skipping any inside strings. Upgrading reindexes bash and C# files once.
- **`Class.method` specs work with `refs --callers`, `callers`, `call-chain`, `impact` and `brief`.** `refs src/shapes.ts::Circle.area --callers` and the other graph commands looked for a symbol literally named `Circle.area`, found nothing and stopped. They now resolve the method inside its class. In TypeScript the callers are narrowed to the ones that call that class's method, so `Square.area`'s callers are left out; in other languages they still match by method name. A Go method's parent is now its receiver type, so `Server.Start` resolves. Upgrading reindexes Go files once.
