- **`changed --symbol` lists only the symbols an edit touched when run from a subfolder or for a file whose path has a space, a quote or a non-ASCII character.** git prints diff paths from the repository root and puts quotes and escapes around unusual names, while the lookup used paths relative to the current folder and expected them unquoted. Nothing matched, so every symbol in the changed file was listed. The diff now runs from the project root with each path passed literally, and quoted names are decoded. A markdown heading now spans its whole section rather than its own line, so `read README.md:8` returns the section that line is in and an edit to a section's body marks that heading as changed. Upgrading reindexes markdown files once.
- **A project kept inside a folder named `build`, `vendor`, `dist` or `node_modules` is indexed.** The skip-directory check looked at every folder in a file's full path, so a project at `C:/work/build/app` or `~/vendor/acme/app` had every file skipped as if it were build output or vendored code, and `symbol`, `read` and `search` found nothing in it. Only folders inside the project count now: `build/` or `node_modules/` within the project is still skipped, and the folders the project sits in are not. The same rule applies to `indexing.skip_dirs`, to the file scan `baseline` makes, and to a walk started with `index . --walk` in a folder that has no project marker.
- **A note anchored to a name that several declarations share is refused instead of silently bound to the first one.** `note set --anchor "a.ts::run"` and `note-add a.ts --symbol run` picked whichever `run` the index listed first, so with `run` defined in two classes the note could follow the wrong method and never flag the change that mattered. Both now stop, list each match with its line, and give the exact retry, such as `--anchor "a.ts::Beta.run"`. A qualified `Class.method` anchor now works, and a bare name still binds without asking when one of the matches is declared at the top level of the file. An overloaded function is tracked as one declaration, signatures and body together, so an edit to the implementation flags the note. A note anchored to an overloaded function before this change shows as changed once, the first time it is listed.
- **A note whose value contains a line or paragraph separator no longer wedges the notes file.** The characters U+2028 and U+2029 were written to the notes file raw, and reading the file back could not parse that line, so the note was lost and later `note set` and `note unset` calls in the project failed. They are now written escaped, and a notes file an older version already wrote this way reads correctly again.
- **A heading inside an HTML comment in a markdown file is no longer treated as a section.** A block such as `<!--` followed by `## Draft` and `-->` hides that heading when the file is rendered, but `outline` listed it, `section` returned it, and the text after the comment was filed under it, so the real section above came back cut short. Headings inside an HTML comment are now skipped the way headings inside a code fence already were. Upgrading reindexes markdown files once.
- **`section` and `skill-section` find a heading that contains `::`.** A request such as `token-goat section "docs/api.md::Foo::bar()"` split at the last `::`, looked for a file named `docs/api.md::Foo`, and failed with "File not found" even though the heading was there. When the text before the last `::` is not a file, the command now tries each earlier `::` and uses the first one that leaves a real file, so C++, Rust and PHP method names work as headings. The MCP server's project-root check now looks at each of those possible files, so a heading with `::` in it cannot be used to read a file outside the project.
- **`token-goat answer` asks who calls a symbol, or what breaks if it changes, about the definition it found.** The answer printed the file it had picked, then asked the graph command about the bare name, so when two files defined the same name, or a copy sat in `node_modules`, the callers listed could belong to a different definition. It now asks about that exact `file::symbol`, and the `via:` line names it. When a name has several definitions in the project, `answer` stops and names the `file::symbol` to ask about instead of guessing, as it already did for explain questions.
- **A PowerShell command that failed just before a `break` or `continue` with a computed label exits 1, as it does in a direct run.** When the label came from a variable or expression, such as `break $l` or `break $null`, and that `break` ended the loop, `token-goat bash` lost the failure of the command right before it, so a script whose last command had failed exited 0. It now keeps that failure and exits 1, on PowerShell 7 and Windows PowerShell 5.1 alike.
- **A PowerShell `break` or `continue` with an empty label now stops only the innermost loop, as it does in a direct run.** PowerShell treats a label that converts to an empty string, such as `$null`, `''`, `""` or `@()`, as no label at all. `token-goat bash` checked such a label against the script's own loop labels, found no match, and sent it past every loop, so the rest of the script never ran and a command that had just failed exited 0. An empty label now stops the nearest loop, and at the top level it ends the script with the exit code a direct run gives.
- **A file you just read is no longer refused as a re-read when its path is typed with different casing on Windows or macOS.** The `hints.protect_recent_reads` setting lets the last few files read this session be read again without a deny or a diff, but the check compared paths case-sensitively while the session remembers each file under the casing of its first read. Reading `src/App.ts` and then `src/app.ts` found the file's session entry but missed its place in the recent list, so the second read was denied as a repeat. The two now match case-insensitively wherever the filesystem ignores case.
- **`token-goat uninstall` no longer crashes when settings.json holds a hook that runs no command.** Claude Code's `prompt`, `agent`, `http` and `mcp_tool` hooks have no `command` field, and uninstall read that field on every hook, so a single such hook made it stop with "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'includes')" and leave token-goat installed. Install, uninstall and doctor now skip any hook without a string command when they look for token-goat's own entries, and leave those hooks exactly as they were.
- **A PowerShell command run through `token-goat bash` exits with the same code as a direct run in three more cases.** A failure in the last statement of a named `begin`, `process` or `clean` block exited 0, where a direct pwsh run exits 1. A `break` with a quoted, dotted or computed label skipped the exit-code check. Under Windows PowerShell 5.1, which is used when pwsh is not installed, a `process` block ran once, though a direct 5.1 run never runs it. All three now match a direct run of the same shell.
- **Two workers starting at once against a dead worker's pid file no longer both run.** When a worker died without removing its pid file, the next two starts each saw the stale file, each deleted it and each created a fresh one, so both believed they held the slot and two workers drained the same queue. Reclaiming a stale pid file now happens under a lock, and only the start that takes the lock removes and recreates the file; the other one sees a live worker and stands down.
- **A file the worker meant to retry is not lost if the worker is killed mid-cycle.** A file whose indexing failed with a passing error such as a locked database is queued for another attempt, but that retry was held in memory until the end of the cycle, after the worker had already deleted the queue file the path came from. A worker killed or crashing between the two dropped the path, and the file's symbols stayed stale until something edited it again. Each retry is now written to a small file beside the queue as soon as it is decided, and the next cycle picks it up; the file is removed once the retries reach the live queue.
- **A hint held for the next tool call no longer arrives with a cut-off piece of an earlier hint in front of it.** On harnesses that drop what the pre-compact hook returns, token-goat holds the recovery manifest and delivers it on the next tool call, in a queue capped at 4,096 bytes. The manifest is sized to fill that cap, so any hint already waiting made the queue overflow, and the cap kept the last 4,096 bytes: the tail of the earlier hint, often half a `token-goat` command, went out ahead of the manifest. When two hints do not fit together, the earlier one is now dropped whole. A single hint longer than the cap keeps its opening lines and is cut at the end of a line.
- **`compress --timeout` reads `1e12` and `2.5` as the numbers they are.** The flag was parsed with `parseInt`, which stops at the first character that is not a digit. `--timeout 1e12` became a 1-second timeout instead of the maximum, and the Bash hook writes a large `timeout_seconds` setting as `1e+21`, so that setting killed every wrapped command after one second. Fractions were cut down too: `0.5` became 0 and fell back to the default. The flag now reads the whole number, rounds a fraction up to the next second, and still caps the value at the largest delay a Node timer accepts.
- **`config-get`, `json-query` and `json-outline` read tsconfig and other JSON files that carry comments.** The read hook suggests these commands for `tsconfig.json`, but the tsconfig `tsc --init` writes has comments and a trailing comma, so all three failed with "Failed to parse JSON" on exactly the file they were suggested for. The same was true of `jsconfig.json`, VS Code settings and `devcontainer.json`. A file that is not strict JSON is now read as JSON with comments when it parses cleanly that way, `config-get` also accepts `.jsonc` files, and a file that is broken either way still reports the same parse error as before.
- **CSV delimiter detection no longer guesses wrong when a quoted field spans lines.** `csv-query` and the other CSV readers sniffed the delimiter from the first 10,000 characters, cut at a line break. When that line break sat inside a quoted field spanning lines, the sample ended in an open quote, the true delimiter failed to parse, and a tab-separated file was read as comma-separated. Detection now samples the first five records whole, however long they are.
- **A shell comment banner made of thousands of dashes no longer hangs `section` or the indexer.** A banner line such as `# ------...` with a title after it was matched by a regex whose cost grew with the cube of the line length: 4,000 dashes took about five seconds and 20,000 ran for over ten minutes. Four other heading readers had the same problem on a smaller scale, slowing with the square of the line when a heading carried a long run of spaces: the large-markdown read hint, `doc-compact --heading`, and the skill-name lookup for loaded skills, which reads both the `Base directory for this skill:` line and the first `#` heading. All five now scan the line once, and a 200,000-character line takes milliseconds. The titles they return are unchanged; each was checked against the old regex on every short string over the characters that decide the match.
- **PowerShell scriptblock execution in `bash_runner` streams output and returns native exit codes.** Calling `[scriptblock]::Create($c).Invoke()` buffered output objects in memory rather than streaming to stdout, wrapped failures in .NET `MethodInvocationException`, and failed on native command pipelines. PowerShell commands now execute with the call operator `& ([scriptblock]::Create($c))`, streaming command output in real time and returning native exit codes directly.
- **`semantic` no longer waits up to 15 seconds when another process is writing the shared database.** After each query, `semantic` records one row of distance figures for `semantic --distances`. That write waited on the same 15-second lock timeout every other database call uses, so a busy worker or a long `index` run could hold the answer back even though the results were already found. Usage counters and the distance ledger now write on their own connection, which gives up after 200 ms and drops the row. A missing telemetry row costs one sample. A stalled query cost the user's time.
- **The retrieval eval no longer scores questions that find their own text.** `evals/retrieval/golden.jsonl` holds every eval question word for word. Once the worker had indexed it, `search` and `semantic` could answer a question by pointing at the fixture line that asks it, and the eval counted that as a hit. Each run of `npm run eval:retrieval` now runs `project exclude` on `evals/retrieval` in the eval's home before asking anything, and it stops with an error naming the questions and files if an arm still returns a hit inside that directory.
- **The retrieval eval reports the weak-match line you actually configured.** The line comparing the fitted `semantic.weak_distance` with the configured one always used the shipped default, so an eval home with a tuned value was scored against a number it never used. The eval now reads the value back with `config get semantic.weak_distance --json`.
- **The session summary token-goat hands over before compaction no longer stops mid-line.** When the summary ran past its length limit, it was cut at that character count, which could split a file path in half and leave a broken line for the summarizer to copy. The notice said how many characters were gone, not which files. The summary is now filled in order of importance: edited files, notes, surgical reads, other reads, then web fetches. Every line is whole, a section that does not fit ends with an exact "and N more", and a section with no room for even one line is named in the notice.
- **Results from token-goat's own MCP tools are no longer fenced as untrusted or denied on a repeat call.** `read`, `symbol`, `section` and the other tools the token-goat MCP server offers read your own workspace, but their results were wrapped as untrusted remote content, which led some models to refuse to act on them, and calling one twice was denied like a repeated web fetch. Third-party MCP servers are still fenced and still deduplicated.
- **On Windows, processes writing the same file no longer lose each other's changes.** Session state, `config set`, project memory and the download-failure record all take a file lock before writing. Three gaps in that lock let two processes in at once, or let one write without it. Windows reports a lock file that is still being deleted as a permission error, which the lock took to mean no lock was possible, so the write went ahead unguarded. A waiter that hit the same error while checking the lock's age could delete a lock another process had just taken. And two waiters that found the same crashed holder could each clear its lock, the second removing the one the first had just created. A retry, an age check that never deletes on error, and a guard that lets only one waiter clear a crashed holder's lock close all three. The retry stops after a second of unbroken refusals, so a folder you genuinely cannot write to still fails fast. Download-failure records now also wait as long as session saves do before giving up.
- **`token-goat upgrade` and `doctor --fix` no longer replace a development checkout, ignore offline mode, or fail on Windows.** The update support added above could install the wrong thing or fail without saying so, and each of these is fixed. The development-checkout check looked at the current directory, so running `upgrade` inside any clone refused even for a global install, and running it anywhere else replaced an `npm link`ed checkout with the registry copy. It now checks the package the running code belongs to, and `doctor` makes the same decision. With `network.offline` set, the check still asked the registry and cached the answer. It now makes no request and writes no cache, and `upgrade` exits 1 and says why, as it does when the registry cannot be reached. A failed check was cached for 24 hours, which hid a new release for a day after one network hiccup, and now expires after an hour. The cache kept announcing an update after it was installed, and now compares against the running version every time it is read. On Windows every upgrade failed, because npm.cmd was started without a shell and Node refuses that with EINVAL. npm now runs as `npm-cli.js` under the current Node. After installing, the new copy resyncs the hooks rather than the old one. The worker and hook servers are stopped before npm replaces files they hold open. A failed install inside `doctor` is reported with the other steps instead of exiting the process partway. The update notice in `doctor`, `stats` and the session-start context said to run `upgrade` even from a development checkout, where it refuses, and while offline, where it checks nothing. It now gives the `git pull` steps for a checkout and stays quiet offline, and `doctor --fix` no longer repeats it after step 7 has reported the update.
- **`doctor`'s instruction-gate check counts the gate `token-goat install` writes.** As first added, the check looked only at the project's own files, so on every machine set up the normal way it warned that the gate was missing. `doctor --fix` then copied the gate into the project's `CLAUDE.md`, creating the file if there was none. Its harness check had the same blind spot and warned about a missing CLI harness in any project with a `.vscode` folder.
- **`.github/hooks/` is git-ignored.** `token-goat install --copilot --local` and `--vscode` write Copilot hooks there, and the commit that taught a test guard to skip the folder described it as ignored when it was not.
- **Files changed by a Codex or opencode `apply_patch` now reach the index.** Codex sends the whole patch as one `command` string, with each file named inside it relative to the directory Codex ran in, and opencode's `apply_patch` tool was not mapped at all. In both cases token-goat never learned which files changed, so `symbol`, `read` and `semantic` kept returning the old code for them until something else touched those files. token-goat now reads the file names out of the patch and queues each one for reindexing.
- **A relative path in a read or edit is now resolved the way the tool resolved it.** Gemini CLI, Copilot CLI, Kimi and opencode can name a file relative to the directory the tool is working in, and token-goat took that path as given, relative to wherever its own hook happened to run. The read was then recorded under a file that did not exist, so a later read of the same file was not recognized as a repeat, and an edit queued the wrong file for reindexing. Hooks now resolve a relative path against the working directory the tool reports. The pi extension also rewrites a path the way pi does before resolving it (a leading `@`, `~`, a `file://` URL, a Git Bash `/c/...` path on Windows, and pi's retry with a narrow space before AM or PM in screenshot names), and resolves it against the directory of the tool call rather than the one the session started in. The OpenClaw plugin resolves paths against the agent's workspace.
- **Gemini CLI and Qwen Code reads that stop short of the end of a file are now recorded as cut short.** Both tools send the file text as it is, with no line numbers, and put a line such as "Showing lines 1-2000 of 2100 total lines" above it when they show only part of a file. token-goat only recognized how Claude Code marks a partial read, so a Gemini or Qwen read that stopped at line 2000 counted as a complete read, and the next read of that file got a plain "already read" note instead of being pointed at `token-goat skeleton` or `token-goat read`. Reads that ask for a range of lines are also placed at the right line numbers now, and a line of the file that happens to start with a number and a tab is no longer taken for a line number.
- **A saved `vite`, `next`, `nuxt`, `remix` or `astro` run is no longer offered back after you change the code.** token-goat saves a dev server's output and suggests reading the saved copy instead of running it again. It is meant to stop suggesting it once the project changes, but it only checked for changes on commands it recognized as builds, and `npx vite`, plain `vite`, `npx next dev`, `npx nuxt dev`, `remix dev` and `astro dev` weren't among them. So after you edited a component, the agent could be pointed at output from before the edit. These commands now go stale as soon as a tracked file changes. `vite-node`, a different tool, is no longer mistaken for vite.
- **A `-c`, `-f` or `-n` inside a file name no longer switches off the Bash read hints.** token-goat checked for `tail`'s follow and byte flags anywhere in the command, so `tail -n 50 docs/appendix-c.md` or `tail -n 50 docs/notes-f.md` was treated as `tail -c` or `tail -f` and got no hint, while `tail -n 50 docs/appendix.md` got one. The same check skipped `tail` reads of a background task's output or a saved tool result when the project folder had `-f` in its name, as Claude Code spells a project at `C:\Projects\f-droid`. The `grep`/`rg` hints had the opposite problem with `-n`: a `-n` inside a name counted as the line-number flag, while the flag written as `grep -rn`, `grep -nH` or `rg --line-number` was missed, so those commands got no hint. Flags are now matched only where they stand as flags.
- **`token-goat install --opencode` on Windows puts the plugin where opencode looks for it.** It wrote the plugin to `%APPDATA%\opencode\plugins\`, but opencode reads its config from `~/.config/opencode` on Windows too (or from `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/opencode` when that is set), so the plugin was never loaded and opencode sessions on Windows ran without token-goat. Install now writes to `~/.config/opencode/plugins/token-goat.ts` and removes the copy an earlier version left under `%APPDATA%`. `uninstall --opencode` removes both. Run `token-goat install --opencode` again to move an existing install.
- **`token-goat config get` no longer says a security setting came from the repository.** A repository's `.token-goat.toml` can't set security settings such as the `[mcp]` section or `hints.fold_code_bodies`, and token-goat throws those lines away. But if the file happened to set one to the value already in effect, `config get` and `config list` labelled it `# from .token-goat.toml`, which suggested the repository was in control. That value is now shown with no label, as coming from your global settings. If the file sets a different value, the output says it isn't in effect and explains why: it's a security setting, and only your global config or an environment variable can set it.
- **token-goat starts on Node 22 when WebAssembly is switched off.** With `--jitless` (in `NODE_OPTIONS`, for example), every command died before doing anything, even `token-goat --version`, with `ReferenceError: WebAssembly is not defined`. The cause was in how token-goat loaded Node's `http` module. On Node 22, loading it that way also loads Node's built-in HTTP client, undici, and undici needs WebAssembly as it starts. 2.9.29 has the same crash. token-goat now loads `http` in a way that does not touch undici, so it starts again. Node 24 was not affected.
- **Compressed pytest output no longer carries the pytest-asyncio and hypothesis header lines.** The pytest filter already dropped pytest's own session header (`platform`, `rootdir:`, `plugins:` and the rest), but plugins add lines of their own to it. With pytest-asyncio installed, every run printed `asyncio: mode=Mode.STRICT, debug=False, ...`; with hypothesis installed, a `-v` run printed `hypothesis profile 'default'`. Both are now dropped. The `Using --randomly-seed=` line from pytest-randomly is kept, because the seed is what lets you rerun an order-dependent failure.
- **A hint is no longer muted because of hints nobody has scored yet.** Each hint gets the next few tool calls to be followed. Until then it has no verdict, and if the session ends first it never gets one. `hint-stats` counted those unscored hints as ignored, and the automatic mute decides on the same numbers. On a real ledger that muted `bash_redirect` at 1 in 11 (9.1%, under the 15% bar), when the hints that had actually been scored stood at 1 in 6 (16.7%). Five of the eleven came from one session that ended mid-window. Unscored hints are now left out of the emitted count, the efficacy figure and the mute decision, and shown beside `emitted` as `+N`, with a footnote saying what they are. For hints that ask the agent not to re-read something, they had been counted as defiance, so they could mute a hint that every scored case showed working. `--json` gains a `pending` field. The `spent-bytes` column also stopped printing `n/a` for a category whose hints were all unscored or unscorable, which had hidden real spend.
- **A hint that works is no longer muted on a handful of unlucky samples.** The automatic mute used to switch a category off once 5 scored hints had been followed less than 15% of the time. Five is too few to tell a useless hint from a useful one having a bad run: a hint followed one time in five in the long run was still muted within 200 hints about 74% of the time. On a real ledger it had muted `bash_recall`, `bash_redirect` and `read_batch` on 7 to 9 scored hints each. The mute now waits until the data shows the follow rate is under 15%. In practice, the highest rate the scored hints still allow has to fall under 15%, measured as the upper end of a 95% confidence range (the Wilson interval). A hint nobody ever follows is muted after 22 scored hints instead of 5, and in exchange a hint followed one time in five is almost never muted (1.4% over 200 hints). For hints that ask the agent not to re-read something, the same test runs on the other side: the mute needs the data to show the agent ignores them more than 85% of the time. `min_sample_size` still sets a floor below which nothing is muted. `npm run eval:hints` prints how often each rule mutes at each follow rate, and with `-- --db <path to global.db>` it reads a ledger read-only and shows each category's range.
- **`search` looks for plain text in every file of a project, not only the first 300.** The text channel stopped after 300 indexed files, and also stopped as soon as it had as many matching lines as the result limit. In this repository that meant it never opened `src/`: `search -c text compressMcpResult` returned one eval fixture and a note saying the scan was capped, while the file that defines the function went unread. Reading all 1,874 indexed files here takes under 200 ms, so the channel now scans every file up to 64 MB in total. A file gives at most three lines, and the files that mention the query most come first, so a changelog full of a name can no longer take every slot from the file that defines it.
- **`token-goat answer` understands more everyday ways of asking.** It used to refuse "where's X defined", "where's the code for X", "show me the source for X", "who uses X", "what uses X", "blast radius of changing X" and "which files depend on some/file.ts", even though it already had an answer for each, because none of those wordings matched. They now route to the same lookup as "where is X defined", "who calls X", "what breaks if I change X" and "what imports some/file.ts". "What depends on X" still goes to the impact lookup. A bare "show me X" is still refused, since it names no question. These wordings came from a set of test questions, and the router refused every one of them.
- **Lookups in a project no longer return copies from git worktrees inside it.** Claude Code creates agent worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/` in the project folder. Each is a full second copy of the project, and token-goat indexed both. So `symbol drainOnce --project .` listed one definition per worktree next to the real one, `search` and `semantic` ranked stale copies against the live file, and `answer` called a plain file name ambiguous. `symbol`, `search`, `semantic`, `answer`, the full-text lookups and the structured-file lookups now leave out any git worktree nested inside the project. Query a worktree as its own project and it still sees its own files. Submodules and monorepo packages are not worktrees and stay in scope. Cleanup is unchanged: `reconcile` still sees the nested rows, so files deleted from a worktree are still removed from the index.
- **`test-for` lists the tests that use a file, not every test that shares a member name with it.** References are recorded by name alone, so a file declaring a common name such as `name`, `start` or `push` was matched by every test using that name anywhere. `test-for src/zip_bounds.ts` listed 605 test files where five import it. When some other source file also defines the name, a test now counts only if it imports the file.
- **A blocked download of the embedding model no longer fails every file one by one.** Before, with the network blocked, a worker draining 30 files asked huggingface.co for the tokenizer 30 times in 25 seconds and logged 60 lines that each said only "fetch failed". Now a download that fails is remembered per host, in one file under the data folder, and the worker, the CLI and the MCP server all read it:
  - Nothing retries that host for 10 minutes. The wait doubles with each further failure, up to an hour.
  - A dropped connection, a 5xx or 429 answer, or a body that breaks off partway is retried twice before it counts as a failure.
  - A name that does not resolve or a certificate the machine does not trust fails at once, since retrying would only repeat it.
  - The error now carries the reason Node hides one level down, such as `connect ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:9`.
  - Loading the model no longer starts a failed download over. It used to go round twice more, printing "Downloading the embedding model" each time, and then reported the wait instead of the error that caused it.
  - `semantic --warm` and `doctor --repair` are you asking to try now, so they skip the wait.
- **`doctor` names the last failed download and how to fix it.** The Embeddings row shows when the model or the WebAssembly runtime binary last failed to download, the error, and what to change: the proxy in `HTTPS_PROXY`, or copying the files into the model folder by hand. It then points at `token-goat doctor --repair`, which now downloads the runtime binary as well as the model.
- **`token-goat index` and `semantic` hand a missing model to the worker when they cannot download it themselves.** That is while a failed download is on hold, or when a proxy is set in `HTTPS_PROXY` that this process would go around. Then `index` skips embedding, says how many files are not embedded yet, and starts the worker, which downloads the model in the background. `semantic` says the model is still on its way and answers with keyword search in the meantime. `semantic --warm` still downloads it in the foreground.
- **Downloads go through the proxy in `HTTPS_PROXY`.** Node's `fetch` ignores that variable unless `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1` was set when Node started, so a machine that reaches the internet only through a proxy could never download the model. `semantic --warm` and `doctor --repair` now restart themselves with the proxy turned on, and the worker is started with it on. This needs Node 22.21 or 24 or later. When a download fails on an older Node with a proxy set, the advice says to upgrade. If the machine reaches huggingface.co and registry.npmjs.org without the proxy, set `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=0` and the downloads go around it.

- **`token-goat waste` and `audit` report on the session you are in.** Run from inside Claude Code, they picked any Copilot CLI session still open in the same project over your own, even one idle for hours, because a live Copilot session always came first. They now take the caller's session by its id (`CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` or `COPILOT_AGENT_SESSION_ID`) and otherwise the most recently written one.

- **`token-goat uninstall --copilot` no longer leaves an empty `copilot-instructions.md` and an empty `hooks` directory behind.** Uninstall stripped token-goat's block and its hook entries but kept the emptied file and folder, so a machine that never had them before ended up with a 0-byte instructions file, an empty `hooks` folder and, for a first install, an empty Copilot home or `.github` folder. Uninstall now removes each of these only when token-goat's own install created it and it is empty. A file or folder that existed before the install, or that now holds your own content, stays. Installs made by earlier versions recorded nothing, so their leftovers stay too and are yours to remove.
- **A second load of a skill is refused in every session, not only the first one that ever loaded it.** token-goat keeps one copy of a skill body that several sessions load unchanged, and a session that loaded a body another session had already stored left no trace of its own. The refusal of a repeat load asks whether this session loaded the skill, so from the second session on it found nothing and the whole body went back into context on every repeat load. Such a session now writes a small record of its load next to the shared copy, and the record goes when that copy is removed.
- **`refs` answers from the current file after reindexing a changed one.** When a file `refs` found had changed on disk, `refs` reindexed it but still printed the rows it had already read, with the old line numbers or a call that no longer exists, and warned that running it again would be current. It now asks the index again after the reindex, so the first answer is the current one. It also reindexes the file named in `file::symbol` before looking, so a call added there since the last index is found. A file that cannot be reindexed is still served with the warning.
- **The worker could be reported as stopped while it was running.** The worker rewrote its liveness file in place, which empties the file for an instant before writing it again. A `worker status` check, or a hook deciding whether to restart the worker, that read the file in that instant saw no process ID and concluded the worker was dead. The file is now written to a temporary name and renamed into place, so a reader always sees a complete file. In a stress test with a second process reading as fast as it could, the old write produced 4,370 empty reads in 1.5 seconds and the new one produced none.
- **`token-goat bridges-status` and the Copilot CLI docs now describe what Copilot CLI 1.0.88 does with hook output, as recorded from real runs.** They said a `postToolUse` hint and a `userPromptSubmitted` hint never reach the model; recorded runs show both do, the first appended to the tool result and the second added to the prompt. They also said a `preCompact` answer could still be used; it is ignored, which is why token-goat hands the compaction manifest back with the next prompt instead. The hook script reads the working directory from `cwd`, the key Copilot CLI sends, after `workingDirectory`, the key its SDK declares, and a test now runs it on a recorded payload.
- **In Copilot CLI, a skill loaded earlier in the session can be recalled with `token-goat skill-body`.** Copilot's skill tool answers with a one-line confirmation and sends the skill's text to the model separately, and token-goat stored that confirmation as the skill. It also looked for the skill file only in Claude Code's skills folder, so `skill-body` and `skill-section` reported the skill as not found, while a second load of the skill was refused with a pointer to those same commands. token-goat now reads the skill from the folders Copilot loads it from (`.github/skills`, `.agents/skills` and `.claude/skills` in the project, then `~/.copilot/skills`, or `$COPILOT_HOME/skills`, and `~/.agents/skills`), and when it finds no file it stores nothing and lets the next load through.
- **After Copilot CLI compacts a conversation, the next prompt carries token-goat's session summary.** Copilot runs its pre-compact hook but ignores what the hook returns (a manual `/compact` on Copilot CLI 1.0.88 showed nothing from it reaching the model), so token-goat queued the summary of files and commands the session touched and sent it with the next tool call. A reply that used no tools never made one, so the summary could wait indefinitely. It now goes out with the next prompt instead, where Copilot does pass hook context to the model, and still goes out with the next tool call if that comes first. Either way it is sent once.
- **Copilot CLI shows each post-tool hint once.** On Copilot CLI 1.0.88 every hint token-goat added after a tool call reached the model twice: once folded into the tool's output and once as the hook's `additionalContext`, which Copilot appends to that output. A hint alone also replaced the tool output with a copy of itself. The hint now goes through `additionalContext` only, and the tool output is replaced only when token-goat really rewrites it.
- **`locate` says when a span comes from a changed or deleted file, and `read "file:N" --json` flags old rows.** `locate` now warns on stderr, tags a deleted file's hits (`⚠ DELETED` in text, `deleted: true` in `--json`), lists live hits first and counts the answer in `stats` like the other read commands. The line-range `read --json` output carries `stale: true` when the file changed and could not be reindexed, matching what its text form already printed.
- **A `symbol` name with no match answers in about a second on a large project, and `--json` gets a JSON reply.** On a 546,000-symbol project a text-mode miss took over five minutes: it read every symbol row, bodies included, in pages that each sorted the whole project again, and when the resident CLI server gave up after 120 seconds the command ran a second time in its own process. It now reads the project's distinct names with one query, finds a name hidden by `--kind` or `--file` with an exact-name lookup, and takes the JSON/YAML candidates from the file table, which brings the same miss to 1.0 to 1.5 seconds with the same `Did you mean` list. A project with more than 500,000 distinct names says the suggestions were skipped rather than ranking part of them. `symbol NAME --json` on a miss used to print a prose line to stderr; it now prints `{"items": [], "truncated": false, "totalCount": 0}` on stdout and exits 0, the shape a hit uses, so it pipes into `jq`. The same names-only query serves the `Did you mean` list after `refs`, `callers`, `call-chain` and `impact` report `Symbol not found`.
- **`find`, `locate` and `symbol --grep` answer in about two seconds on a large project instead of about three minutes.** They read every symbol in the project to match names, and did so in pages that each sorted the whole project again, bodies included, before skipping to their place. On a 546,000-symbol project `find` took 166 seconds, `locate` 168 and `symbol --grep` 195. They now read in pages that each start where the last one ended, in the order of the file-path index, and read only names, paths and line spans, fetching a body only for a row `symbol` prints. The same three commands take 1.6 to 1.8 seconds there, with byte-identical output. `symbol --exclude-tests` and `--exclude-vendored` read the same way.
- **A read-only command the resident server takes now runs once, however long it runs.** The command waited 120 seconds for the server's answer, then gave up and ran the whole command again in its own process. The server cannot stop a command it has started, so a slow one ran twice at once on the same index, and the second run was never faster than the first: a 342.8-second `symbol` miss on a large project was 120 seconds of waiting and then the whole command again. The command now waits for the server's answer. It still runs in its own process when the server fails partway through, which ends the connection: with the server killed 0.7 seconds into a `symbol --grep`, the command answered on its own 2.4 seconds later. Hook calls keep their 120-second limit.
- **`symbol` lists a live definition ahead of one from a deleted checkout, and `refs` marks call sites in deleted files.** The index keeps a missing project's rows for a week in case its disk is only unmounted, and in that time a removed worktree such as `proj-wt/` sorted ahead of `proj/`, so the first definition shown was the dead copy. `refs` listed those call sites with no marker and said the files had changed on disk and would be current on a repeat. Both now tag each such row `⚠ DELETED` (`deleted: true` in `--json`), put it after the live ones, and `refs` says the files are no longer on disk.
- **`waste` and `audit` with no `--transcript` pick the newest session for the project, and a finished Copilot CLI session no longer counts as running.** Copilot CLI leaves an empty lock file under `session-state/.session-operation-locks` after a session exits, and its presence was read as a live session, so a days-old Copilot log was chosen over the Claude Code transcript being written that minute. Only a lock that names a running process now marks a session live. The `--transcript` help says which transcript is chosen by default.
- **File specs accept `~/` for the home directory, and `section` accepts a Git Bash `/c/...` path.** A quoted `~/notes.md::Heading` reaches token-goat with the tilde intact, and no command expanded it, so it was looked up as a directory named `~`. `section` also read its file without the drive translation `read` and `outline` apply, so `section "/c/Projects/x.md::Heading"` answered "File not found". Every file-spec command now resolves the path the same way, including their MCP tools, whose project-root check measures the expanded path. `~user` is left as written.
- **Notes named `__proto__`, `constructor`, `toString` or `hasOwnProperty` work like any other note.** `note set __proto__ ...` saved nothing, and `note get` answered with `[object Object]`. `note get constructor` printed `function Object() { [native code] }` for a note that was never set. A new note named `constructor`, `toString` or `hasOwnProperty` did not count toward the 30-note limit, so the file could grow past it. `note list --json` now includes a `__proto__` note too.
- **Grok hooks on Windows run.** The Node form of each Grok hook entry was a PowerShell parse error, so every Grok hook did nothing and reported nothing. The entry is now a call-operator line. Reinstall with `token-goat install --grok` to rewrite the entries.
- **Hook entries that Codex, Grok and Copilot CLI run through PowerShell keep the command's exit code.** A nonzero exit used to be collapsed to success on the way out, and an entry whose binary was missing exited 0. Both now exit with the right code, and a missing binary exits 1.
- **A hook server no longer retires itself in the middle of a request.** A status contact arriving while a request was running was judged by that request's swapped-in environment, so a caller without `TOKEN_GOAT_HOOK_SERVER` set could make a server started with it read itself as disabled and exit. Whether the server is enabled is now decided only between requests.
- **`doctor` no longer reports hook events as missing when an older token-goat command is still delivering them.** Settings written by an earlier build were read as lacking every Claude Code event, followed by "Those events never reach token-goat", while each of those events was still firing. `doctor` now tells the two apart. An event with no working token-goat hook is reported as missing, as before. An event wired to an older command that still runs is reported as wired to an older command, and `token-goat install` rewrites it in both cases. An older command counts as working only if the shim file it names still exists.
- **A `TaskOutput` or `BashOutput` poll that adds a little to a long output now returns only the new part.** The size check that decides whether to trim a repeat poll measured the new text alone, so a poll adding less than `bash_compress.cache_min_bytes` (512 bytes by default) to a long output sent the whole output again. It now measures the output being replaced, as it already did for a poll with nothing new.
- **A saved failing `npm test`, `yarn test` or `pnpm test` run is no longer offered in place of a rerun after the code changes.** Where the harness reports exit codes, as Grok does, a failing run of these was saved with no record of the repository state, so a rerun after an edit was told to re-inspect the saved output instead and went out uncompressed. The saved run now lapses when the commit or `git status` changes, as a `vitest`, `jest` or `pytest` run already did.
- **A malformed hook-server handshake no longer ends the process that receives it.** A MAC as long as the real one in characters but longer in bytes, which a single non-ASCII character is enough for, made the comparison throw instead of fail. A connection sending one ended the hook server, and a command such as `section` that got one back from whatever was listening at the server's address exited with an error. Such a MAC now fails like any other wrong one: the server hangs up on that connection and keeps serving, and the command answers from its own process.
- **Commands whose names only begin with `jest` or `vitest`, such as `jest-codemods`, `jest-preview` and `vitest-preview`, are no longer offered their saved output in place of a rerun.** They matched the patterns for jest and vitest runs, so a repeat was told its earlier output was cached and given a grep for test results. Since they are not test runs, that saved output also never lapsed when the code changed. The patterns now stop where the runner's name ends, as test-run detection already did.
- **`vite-node`, `clippy-driver` and `nuxt devtools` runs are no longer offered their saved output in place of a rerun.** They matched the patterns for `vite`, `clippy` and `nuxt dev`, so a repeat was told its earlier output was cached, and since none of them is treated as a build, that saved output never lapsed when the code changed. The patterns now stop at the end of the command name.
- **Compressed `turbo` output keeps the run summary after a task replayed from cache.** `turbo` indents the `Tasks:` and `Time:` lines to line up with `Cached:`, so none of the three was recognized as the summary, and when the last task was a cache hit they were dropped along with its replayed log.
- **With `hints.cross_session_read_dedup` on, a file saved with a byte-order mark, or as UTF-16 or UTF-32, counts as unchanged when it is.** Its recorded text was decoded by the mark and the later checks read it as plain UTF-8, so the two never matched: a Read of the file got no notice that an unchanged copy was on record, and every session start listed it as changed. A file too large to record is no longer read in full to check it.
- **Lowering `indexing.max_chunks_per_file` now applies to files already in the index.** The sweep that removes the chunks of files over the limit ran once per token-goat version, so after the limit was lowered, a file kept all its chunks until its content changed or token-goat was upgraded. The sweep now runs again whenever the limit differs from the one it last applied.
- **`refs` with several symbols in one spec, such as `a.ts::run,stop` or `a.ts::run,b.ts::run`, lists only the references to the definition each one names.** Those forms looked up a relative file as written rather than as the index stores it, so no definition was found, and a call to any symbol of that name anywhere in the project was listed as a reference. They now resolve the file the way the single-symbol form does.
- **A `bash-output`, `web-output` or `mcp-output` recall comes back in full when the session has already seen some of its lines.** Such a recall was itself shortened to a notice pointing at the output being recalled, so following the notice produced the same notice again. A recall is now delivered as printed, whatever its `--full`, `--grep` or `--section` flags.
- **Starting the worker no longer sends a stop signal to the process its old pid file names.** A worker killed before it could clean up leaves its pid file behind, and once the operating system gave that pid to another program, the next start sent that program SIGTERM, which on Windows ends a process outright. The start now takes over the pid file without signaling anything, and a worker still running after losing the file stops on its own at its next poll.
- **`doctor` counts a file queued for reindexing several times as one pending file, as the worker drains it.** The Dirty queue row counted queue lines, so a file edited three times while no worker ran showed as three pending files, and a burst of edits to a few files could raise the backlog warning.
- **Saved output keeps every line after an empty secret-named assignment such as `API_KEY=`, and stays readable with `redaction.strict` on.** Output saved for `bash-output`, `mcp-output`, `web-output`, `retrieve` and `handoff-resolve`, and the session state hooks save, was checked for secrets as JSON text, where a line break is written `\n`. An empty value took that `\n` and the text after it into its mask, up to the next space or quote, which in an environment listing can be every line that follows. In strict mode, a line that starts with a long run of capitals and digits, such as an uppercase SHA-256 digest, could be masked together with the `n` before it, which left the saved record unreadable, so its id was reported as not found. The same happened to such a run right after an unpaired surrogate, such as half of an emoji that was cut in two. The check now matches against the text itself, with line breaks read as line breaks.
- **The background worker no longer keeps the directory it was started from in use.** It inherited the working directory of whichever hook started it, and on Windows a process's working directory cannot be deleted, so a hook run inside a temporary copy of a project left that copy undeletable until `token-goat worker stop`. The worker now starts in the temp directory, and moves there itself when started any other way. The hook server is started there too, rather than moving there only once it is running. One side effect: the worker used to read a per-project `.token-goat.toml` from the directory it inherited and apply it to every project it indexed. It now takes its own settings, such as `worker.max_pool_workers`, from your own config alone.
- **`indexing.embeddings_enabled` in a project's `.token-goat.toml` now decides whether the background worker embeds that project's files.** The worker applied the `.token-goat.toml` of whichever directory it happened to start in to every project it indexed, so whether a project's files were embedded depended on where the worker had been started, and its idle pass embedded the files that `token-goat index` had just left unembedded in a project that turned embeddings off. It now reads the setting for each file from that file's project, or from the nearest enclosing project with a `.token-goat.toml`, so one at the root of a monorepo covers its packages.
- **`token-goat index` decides whether to embed a file from that file's own project, as the background worker does.** It read `indexing.embeddings_enabled` from the project of the directory it ran in, so run inside a monorepo package, or given a path from another directory, it embedded files that a `.token-goat.toml` at the repository root turns off, and the worker's next pass stamped them unembedded again. Both now read the setting from the file's project, or from the nearest enclosing project with a `.token-goat.toml`.
- **`token-goat reconcile`, and the sweep at session start, notice a file whose name changed only in case.** On Windows and macOS, `git mv beta.ts Beta.ts` leaves the file's content and modification time as they were, so the sweep reported the project clean and `symbol` went on answering with `beta.ts`. The sweep now queues such a file, and the background worker records it under its new name.
- **A background worker that fails to start no longer crashes the hook that tried to start it.** When an edit or index hook restarted the worker and the new process could not start, Node reported why after the start call had returned, where nothing caught it, and the hook crashed. The hook now carries on, the failure goes to `worker-errors.log` as before, and the next health check tries again.
- **In Claude Code, a Read of a file with lines that begin with a number and a tab is folded at the lines its notices name.** Such a line, a TSV record or a `cat -n` listing kept in a string, was taken for a line number, so the fold worked from the wrong lines: it could withhold part of the listing under a notice naming a function whose body was shown in full, or leave the whole file unfolded. Every line is now numbered by its position in the file, as Claude Code numbers it.
- **In pi, an edit or write to a file named by a relative path now reaches the index.** pi's edit and write tools accept a path relative to the session directory, and the edit hook queued it as given. The background worker runs in the temp directory, where that path names nothing, so the edit was never indexed and `symbol` and `semantic` kept answering from the file as it was before the edit. The hook now queues the absolute path.
- **`token-goat reconcile` and the session-start note no longer call a file queued for reindexing when the queue turned it away.** The queue accepts nothing under the system temp directory, and nothing at all when it cannot be written, but the sweep counted every file it handed over. `reconcile --json` reported those files as `enqueued`, the text report said they were queued, and the note at session start told the model token-goat was reindexing them. Both now count only what reached the queue, say how many files did not, and point at `token-goat index` to bring the index up to date.
- **The Read that a folded comment's notice names now returns the withheld lines after a partial read of the file.** A Read with `offset` and `limit`, or a `sed -n` or `head` read of the file, put every line it covered on record as read before the fold withheld some of them, so the notice's Read was refused as lines already read. A read whose output is folded now takes that record back.
- **A file read in Bash after `cd DIR &&` is recorded as read in DIR.** A `head`, `cat`, `tail` or `Get-Content` read behind that prefix was put on record against the same-named file in the directory the command started from. After `cd sub && head -n 40 x.ts`, a Read of `./x.ts` was refused as lines already read, though only `sub/x.ts` had been shown, and a Read of `sub/x.ts` found no record of it. A `curl -o` download behind the same prefix was recorded at that same wrong path whenever a file of that name existed there, so repeating the download was refused as already saved to a file holding something else. Both are now recorded where the `cd` left the shell.
- **`mcp-output --json-query --json` prints JSON that parses.** Its whole output was wrapped in the notice that marks recalled tool output as untrusted, so parsing it always failed. A string that reads like an instruction to the model is now wrapped where it sits, and secrets are masked in keys and values as before.
- **On Windows, `doctor` finds token-goat and `install` reads the Claude Code version when npm's global directory has a space in its path, as it does under an account name with a space.** Both ran the `.cmd` shim npm puts there in a way that cut the shim's path at the first space. `doctor` reported `token-goat command not found`, and `install` wrote hooks in the string form, which Claude Code runs through a shell, instead of the form it runs directly.
- **Saved Bash output is offered in place of a rerun only in the directory it came from.** Output was saved under the command alone, so after `cd pkgA && cargo build`, a later `cd pkgB && cargo build` was told pkgA's output was cached. When pkgB's build ran anyway, its output was taken for a rerun of pkgA's: pkgA's errors were reported as resolved, and the compaction manifest marked pkgA's output as replaced. Output is now saved under the directory the command runs in, which is where its `cd` prefix leads when it has one.
- **`token-goat read` and `token-goat section` run in Bash after `cd DIR &&` are matched against the file in DIR.** The check for a file already read with the Read tool looked at the same-named file in the directory the command started from. In a subagent, whose shell starts every command in the same directory, so did the record of the query: after `cd sub && token-goat read x.ts::foo` there, a query of `./x.ts` was told it had already been run while a repeat naming `sub/x.ts` was not.
- **A file rewritten by a command behind `cd DIR &&` is queued for reindexing in DIR.** Where the shell was still in DIR when the command ended, as on Claude Code's main thread, a `sed -i`, `tee` or redirect target was looked for at `DIR/DIR/file`. Where it was not, as in a subagent or when token-goat ran the command itself to compress its output, a `git restore` path was looked for in the directory the command started in, and when DIR was in another repository, the files changed by a `git checkout`, `git pull`, `git stash pop` or a formatter were looked for in the repository the command started in. Those files were never queued, so `symbol`, `read` and the other indexed commands kept serving their old content.
- **A package script run behind `cd DIR &&`, such as `cd packages/a && npm test`, is compressed with the filter for the test runner named in DIR's own `package.json`.** Both Bash hooks looked the script up in the `package.json` of the directory the command started from rather than the one the `cd` leads to. In a workspace whose packages use different test runners, a package's run started from the root was compressed for whatever the root's `test` script named, or matched to no runner at all when the root has no `test` script. The hooks now look the script up in the directory the command runs in.
- **`token-goat waste` no longer lists a repeated Bash command behind a `cd` prefix as missing the cache when its output was saved.** The report looked each call up under the command as the transcript records it, while the Bash hooks save output under the command without its `cd` prefix, leading variable assignments, subshell parentheses or `token-goat compress` wrapper, and under the directory it runs in. A command such as `cd crates/core && cargo build`, the usual way to build in a subdirectory, was listed under "Repeated Bash commands not hitting the token-goat cache" whether or not its output had been saved. The report now looks each call up the way the hooks save it.
- **Uninstalling from Claude Code, Gemini CLI or Qwen Code deletes token-goat's settings backups even when its hooks were already removed.** Install keeps timestamped copies of the `settings.json` it edits, and uninstall deleted them only when it had just taken token-goat's hook entries out. After the entries had been removed by hand or by an earlier uninstall, every backup stayed, and when Claude Code's settings file had no `hooks` section at all, its uninstall also left token-goat's hook script behind. These three now clean up whatever is left, as the Codex and OpenClaw uninstalls already did, and still delete nothing but token-goat's own files.
- **`token-goat uninstall` removes the empty `CLAUDE.md`, `hooks` directory and `skills` directory that token-goat's install created in Claude Code's config directory.** Taking out token-goat's `CLAUDE.md` block, hook script and skill used to leave behind the file and directories install had made to hold them. Install now records each one it creates, and uninstall removes it once nothing is left in it. One that existed before token-goat was installed stays, however empty, and so does one the user has since put anything into. An install made by an earlier version recorded nothing, so what it created stays as well.
- **A Bash read of a file that is not there is no longer recorded as read.** A command such as `cat x.ts | head -n 40` that found no `x.ts`, or a `sed -n` range of a missing file, still counted as showing those lines, so once the file appeared through a checkout, a build or a copy, a later read of them was told they had already been served.
- **The large-output hint's `token-goat compress -c` suggestion keeps the `cd` of a command run behind `cd DIR &&`.** Without it, the suggested command ran wherever the shell happened to be. The suggestion now starts with a `cd` to the full path of DIR. When DIR cannot be found, the hint offers only the `bash-output` recall.
- **Output that the Bash hooks compress or keep after a piped or compound command behind `cd DIR &&` is saved under DIR.** It was saved under the directory the call started in, which is the directory a Claude Code subagent reports, so `token-goat waste` listed such a command as missing the cache even though its output had been saved.
- **`token-goat uninstall` leaves a Claude Code, Gemini CLI or Qwen Code `settings.json` that it cannot read or parse as it is, names the file, and exits 1.** Uninstall used to treat such a file as holding no token-goat hooks, so the Claude Code uninstall deleted the hook script the file's hooks still ran, and all three deleted the file's backups. The file, the hook script and the backups now stay until the file is fixed and uninstall runs again. Uninstalling one Claude Code scope also keeps the shared hook script while the other scope's file cannot be read or parsed. Install now names an unreadable settings file and refuses it, as it already did a file that does not parse.
- **`section --list`, `image-meta` and `image-text` report a directory given in place of a file as unreadable, as they do a missing file.** `section --list` used to answer "No sections found" for a document it never read, and the image commands printed the raw error from reading a directory.
- **Under WSL, a project on a Windows drive works.** token-goat recorded a file under `/mnt/c` by its drive-letter name, `c:/x` for `/mnt/c/x`, and on Linux that name is a relative path to nothing. `index --walk` in such a project indexed 0 files, so the read commands found nothing there and `outline` answered "Could not read". The hooks took the file for missing too: a Read of an 84 KB log under `/mnt/c` went through in full, where a Read of the same log under `/var/tmp` is blocked, as large files are, and the checks behind read hints, saved snapshots, `curl -o` downloads and edited files passed it over. Under WSL a file is now recorded at its `/mnt` path, so the project indexes, `outline`, `read` and `symbol` answer, and the worker reindexes a file after an edit. A file an earlier version recorded under its drive-letter name is still opened at its `/mnt` path. Windows still records the drive letter. The parser is unchanged, so nothing is reparsed on upgrade.
- **`token-goat index` finds the folder you name when you spell it the way another shell would.** On Windows, a folder typed as `/mnt/c/x` or `/c/x` (in PowerShell, cmd, or a Git Bash script run with `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1`) was looked for at `C:\mnt\c\x`, which does not exist, and `index --walk` printed `Indexed 0 files` and exited as if it had worked. Under WSL, `C:\x` had the same result. The folder is now opened where this machine keeps it, so all three spellings index it. A folder that does not exist at all is now reported as not existing, with exit code 1, where before `--walk` reported `Indexed 0 files` and a plain `index` said it was not a git repository.
- **A `cat` or `Get-Content` read of a file with a hyphen in its name is taken as a read of that file.** Part of the name was taken for one of the command's parameters. `Get-Content docs/loop-ledger.md -TotalCount 40` was taken as a read of `docs/loop.md`, so the hint named a file the command never read and the size check measured that file instead. A whole-file `cat` or `Get-Content`, or a `Get-Content -Tail`, of a name holding `-head`, `-tail`, `-first` or `-totalcount`, such as `precision-first-low-promotion/SKILL.md`, got no hint and was not recorded as read.
- **Uninstalling from Codex, Kimi Code, OpenClaw or JetBrains leaves a config file it cannot read or parse as it is, names the file, and exits 1.** Codex's and Kimi Code's `config.toml` and OpenClaw's `openclaw.json` were read as holding no token-goat entries when they could not be read or parsed, so uninstall deleted the hook script, `AGENTS.md` block, skill or plugin the file might still use, and the file's backups. JetBrains passed over an `mcp.json` it could not parse and removed the Copilot instructions block anyway. Each now stops before changing anything, and runs in full once the file reads again. OpenClaw also refuses a `plugins.load.paths` that is not a list, which it used to drop from the file on saving it. JetBrains uninstall now deletes the backups it made of `mcp.json`. Install for Codex, Kimi Code and OpenClaw names an unreadable config file and refuses it, as it already did one that does not parse, and OpenClaw's install does so before it writes its plugin.
- **A command behind `cd ~/DIR &&` is tied to DIR in the home directory.** An unquoted `~` or `~/...` in the `cd` was taken as a folder named `~` under the directory the command started from, so the output saved for such a command, the checks for a repeat of it and the record of the files it read all named a directory that does not exist, and a later run of the same command in that directory, or a Read of a file there, found none of them. `~` now means the home directory, as it does to bash, and the large-output hint's `compress -c` suggestion keeps the `cd`. `~user`, a quoted `~` and a variable are still taken as written.
- **A failed write while switching embedding runtimes no longer leaves old and new vectors mixed.** When the database was busy at the moment token-goat noticed the embedding runtime had changed, the check was remembered as done even though the new runtime was never recorded, so a long-running worker went on comparing new query vectors against old stored ones. The reset and the record of the new runtime are now written together, and a failed write is retried on the next use.
- **`THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` names the exact release of every package the bundle includes, nested copies too.** It looked each package up by name at the top of `node_modules`, so it listed the wrong `onnxruntime-common` release and left out the `entities` 7.0.1 a parser brings along.
- **An archived changelog entry gave `onnxruntime-node`'s installed size as 34 MB.** It is about 288 MB, every platform's binaries included.
- **Front matter at the top of a markdown file is no longer indexed as a heading.** A Jekyll block like the one that opens README.md ends in a `---` line, and the heading scanners took that line for the underline of a heading. The last metadata key (`permalink: /` in README.md) then showed up as a level 2 heading ahead of the real title in `outline`, `section` and read hints. The scanners now start after the front matter. An opening `---` with no closing one is not treated as front matter.
- **`token-goat section --grep <pattern>` filters the section.** The flag was accepted and then ignored, so the whole section came back. It now prints only the matching lines, each under the sub-heading it sits in.
- **`token-goat symbol NAME` lists the current project's definitions first.** Matches were sorted by path across every indexed project, so another project whose path sorted earlier could fill the page before the one you were working in appeared. Definitions from the current project now come first, and a truncated page says how many of the matches are local.
- **Search keeps far-apart hits separate and points at the matching line.** Two hits on the same name hundreds of lines apart were merged into one range covering everything between them. Hits now merge only when they are within 20 lines of each other, and a match inside a large function prints its own line (for example `cli.ts:923`) instead of the whole function's range.
- **`bootstrap-audit --project` counts the project's own agents and skills.** It looked only in `~/.claude`, so agents and skills under the project's `.claude` folder were never counted. Each entry is now labelled `user` or `project`. Only `<name>/SKILL.md` counts as a skill, which is the layout Claude Code loads. A loose markdown file directly in a skills folder is reported as ignored, and reference files inside a skill's folder no longer add to the total.
- **`map` and subagent briefings rank top symbols by references from the same language.** References are stored by bare name, so every TypeScript `path.join(` or `.push(` call counted toward a Rust helper of the same name, and those helpers took top slots in the map that every subagent briefing carries. A reference now counts only when it comes from a file in the same language family (TypeScript and JavaScript count as one), and calls from inside the defining file no longer count. The recent-files list always uses forward slashes.
- **`token-goat stats` lists the files the current session read.** Run inside Claude Code, the top-files block showed whichever session had written last, which could be a different window. It now uses the current session and its subagents. Without a session id it falls back to the newest session and labels the block that way.
- **`read` returns the whole rule or function in CSS, SCSS, Less, Vue, Svelte and Astro files.** A multi-line CSS rule was stored as its selector line, and a function, const or class in a component's script block as its first line, so `read file.css::.btn` returned the selector with no declarations and `outline` showed one line for a four-line function. Each now runs to its closing brace or bracket. Braces inside strings and comments are ignored, SCSS and Less `//` comments included, and a commented-out selector is not indexed.
- **`doctor --repair` no longer saves environment variables or project settings into the global config.** It built its fix from the effective config and saved all of it, so a one-off `TOKEN_GOAT_*` variable, or the current repository's `.token-goat.toml`, became permanent in `config.toml`. Repair now edits only what the file itself holds, and lists a setting held by a variable or a project file under "Left unchanged" with its source named. The model and WebAssembly downloads are skipped while offline mode or disabled embeddings is in force.
- **Offline mode without the embedding model gets one clear explanation.** `index` said the background worker would download the model and suggested the warm command, both of which offline mode forbids, and `doctor` warned twice about the same missing model. `index` now names the setting that holds offline mode, says `symbol`, `read`, `outline` and `section` still work, and gives the folder the model files can be copied into. Doctor warns once.
- **`install` says why it skipped a temp folder or a home or drive root.** Both printed "No project here to index", which was wrong. Each message now names the folder and the reason: a temp folder can still be indexed by running `token-goat index` in it, and a home or drive root needs a specific project instead.
- **A compaction resets subagents' dedup state too.** A subagent's compaction event carries no agent id, so only the main thread's served-lines, repeat-deny and already-ran notes were cleared, and subagents kept withholding output they could no longer see. Every subagent of a compacted session now starts over, and the main thread no longer says it already ran a query from before the compaction.
- **The resume packet and compaction manifest show an expired page as its URL.** Each fetched page was listed with a cache id and its full prompt after the cached body was gone. A list where every page expired now collapses to one line, and an empty "Recent bash commands" heading is no longer printed.
- **token-goat run as `node .../token-goat.mjs` or `npx token-goat` is recognised as token-goat.** Only a first word of `token-goat` or `tg` counted, so a recall run any other way was withheld as already-served output and its piped output was clipped.
- **The large-output hint no longer suggests compressing a line-range read.** A piped or chained `sed -n` read already prints only the lines asked for, so `token-goat compress` had nothing to save.
- **A ranged Read of new lines no longer gets an "already read" note.** When the window overlapped nothing read earlier in the session, the note was wrong, and a hint was counted for it anyway.
- **The monitoring recall hint only names a cached run that holds the slice you asked for.** It could point at a run cached with a different `head`, `tail` or `sed` window and count a saving for it. The trailing space it left in the command summary is gone too.
- **A line just over the length cap is left whole.** A line one character over lost that character and grew by about 21 bytes for the cut marker. Lines are now cut only when more than 64 characters would go.
- **`sqlite-schema`, `sqlite-tables` and `describe` work on databases with a sqlite-vec table.** They failed with "no such module: vec0", token-goat's own index databases included, and showed no tables at all. A virtual table whose module is not loaded is now listed as virtual with its CREATE statement, and the other tables are described as usual.
- **Command-line errors print once.** A mistyped option was reported twice, once by the argument parser and once by token-goat.
- **Read hints for JSON, YAML and lock files now suggest a command that works on them.** The lock-file deny and the `package.json`, `tsconfig` and manifest hints offered `token-goat section`, which only slices Markdown-style headings and prints "has no headings" for these files. They now name `json-query`, `yaml-query`, `config-get`, `xml-outline` or a plain search, depending on the file type, and lock files are recognised by name. The `package.json` hint also names the path you read rather than a bare `package.json`.
- **On Windows, the session summary written before compaction includes the `mem` epoch again.** The probe started `mem` directly, and that cannot launch the `.cmd` wrapper npm and cargo install on Windows, so the section was dropped without a word. It now goes through the same shim-aware launcher token-goat uses for other tools, and still leaves the section out when `mem` is missing or fails.
- **Two compactions in a row no longer deliver two recovery summaries.** On harnesses that drop pre-compact context, the summary waits for the next tool call. A second compaction before that call queued a second summary behind the stale first one, and together they could overflow the queue and cut off the start of the newer one. The newer summary now replaces the older one, and any other hint already queued is kept.
- **`insert-section` leaves the rest of a file with mixed line endings alone.** It rejoined the whole file with one line ending, so a file mixing CRLF and LF had every line rewritten. Only the inserted lines change now, and they take the line ending of the line before them.
- **`replace` no longer overwrites an edit that lands while it reads the file.** It took its stale-write snapshot after reading the file, so a change made in between became the starting point and was lost. The snapshot is now taken first.
- **`todo --kinds` with a trailing comma no longer reports every line.** An empty entry matched every line. Empty entries are now dropped, and a value with nothing left falls back to the default kinds.
- **`section --grep --max-lines` prints a header range that matches the output.** The range was cut to the first lines of the section even though the printed lines were matches from anywhere in it. With `--grep`, the range now covers the span the matches came from; `--max-lines` on its own still narrows it.
- **Wide TSV files and short first rows read correctly in the CSV commands.** Delimiter detection sampled a fixed 10,000 characters, which can stop partway through a row on a wide file. Detection then fell back to a comma, and a 300-column TSV read as one column. The sample now ends on a whole row. Column names now come from the header line rather than the first record, so a short first data row no longer hides columns from `--columns`; without a header, the widest row sets the column count.
- **Large UTF-32 files can be decoded.** The decoder built the string in a single call, which throws once the file holds a few hundred thousand characters. It now works in chunks.
- **One very long line no longer stalls indexing for a minute.** The patterns that find Markdown headings, TOML and INI table headers and CSS selectors slowed down sharply on a long run of spaces, so a single 500 KB line could hold up the shared worker for about a minute. Hand-written matchers that run in linear time replace them, each checked against the old pattern over every short input built from an edge-case alphabet.
- **PowerShell commands run through token-goat report the same exit code as `pwsh` itself.** A failing cmdlet exited 0, and a native command that failed earlier in the script could make a script that succeeded exit non-zero. The exit code now follows the rule `pwsh -Command` uses: 1 when the last statement failed, 0 otherwise, and the number given to `exit`. A native command that fails as the last statement exits 1, as it does under `pwsh`, not with its own code. A script that ends early with a top-level `return` exits with the result of the statement before it; it used to exit 1 every time. An error that only stops one statement, such as an unknown command or a .NET exception, lets the rest of the script run, as it does under `pwsh`. A `try`/`finally`, a `return` with a value, a top-level `break` and a `break` naming a label nothing catches all exit as `pwsh` does, and a command that does not parse exits 1 with a ParserError that quotes the line, where `pwsh` exits 1 and prints nothing.
- **A shell that cannot be started is reported as exit 127.** When the shell binary was missing, one path returned 0 and the other crashed token-goat. Both now print the error and exit 127, the code shells use for a command that was not found.
- **A very large `--timeout` no longer kills the command at once.** Values above 2,147,483 seconds overflowed Node's timer limit, and Node treats that as one millisecond. The timeout is now capped at that value.
- **The MCP server picks the right project when a file name contains `_` or `%`.** To guess the project root from a file name, it compared names with SQL `LIKE`, where those characters are wildcards, and took whichever of five matches came back first. Names are now compared literally, and the shortest matching path wins.
- **Notes set through paths that differ only in letter case no longer split into two notes.** On a case-insensitive drive, setting a note for `Src/A.ts` and then for `src/a.ts` made two rows, and reading returned the older one. Writes now match the path the way reads do, and they also update duplicates already in an existing store, so nothing needs migrating.
- **The worker no longer reads a file it is about to skip.** A file over `indexing.large_file_skip_kb` was read whole on every change just to be skipped. A file over 2 GiB could not be read at all, so the worker retried it until it gave up and left the file's old symbols in the index. The worker now checks the path and size first, as `index` does, and removes the file's rows.
